\displaystyle\|\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{S}(A^{k}_{s,t}-A_{s,t})\|_{L_{n}}\leqslant C\Gamma_{1}\eta_{S,T}^{(\alpha_{1},\beta_{1})}(s,t)(t-s)^{\varepsilon_{1}}\,.

(4.20)

Sending
k\to\infty
and reasoning as previously, we obtain (
4.6
).
∎

Proof of
4.4
.

The proof goes along exactly the same lines as the proof of
4.1
till (
4.19
). Rewriting this inequality, we derive

\|\|A^{k}_{s,t}\|_{L_{m}|\mathcal{F}_{S}}\|_{L_{n}}\leqslant\|\|A_{s,t}\|_{L_{m}|\mathcal{F}_{S}}\|_{L_{n}}C\Gamma_{1}\eta_{S,T}^{(\alpha_{1},\beta_{1})}(s,t)(t-s)^{\varepsilon_{1}}+C\Gamma_{2}|\eta_{S,T}^{(2\alpha_{2},2\beta_{2})}(s,t)|^{\frac{1}{2}}(t-s)^{\varepsilon_{2}}.

By passing to the limit as
k\to\infty
and using (
4.8
) and Fatou’s lemma, we obtain (
4.9
). Inequality (
4.10
) follows similarly from (
4.20
), (
4.8
) and Fatou’s lemma.
∎

Proof of
4.5
.

The term
J^{k}_{s,t}
is estimated as in the proof of Theorem
4.1
, which gives

\|J^{k}_{s,t}\|_{L_{m}}\leqslant 2^{-k\varepsilon_{2}+2}\kappa_{m}\Gamma_{2}(t-s)^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon_{2}}.

(4.21)

On the other hand, we estimate
I^{k}_{s,t}
differently, using triangle inequality and condition (
4.11
) in the following way

\displaystyle\|I^{k}_{s,t}\|_{L_{m}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{i=0}^{2^{k}-1}\|\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{t^{k}_{i}}\delta A_{t^{k}_{i},u^{k}_{i},t^{k}_{i+1}}\|_{L_{m}}\leqslant\Gamma_{3}\sum_{i=0}^{2^{k}-1}(t^{k}_{i+1}-t^{k}_{i})+\Gamma_{4}\sum_{i=0}^{2^{k}-1}(t^{k}_{i+1}-t^{k}_{i})^{1+\varepsilon_{4}}

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{3}(t-s)+2^{-k\varepsilon_{4}}\Gamma_{4}(t-s)^{1+\varepsilon_{4}}\,.

Hence, in view of (
4.16
), we have shown that for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}

\|A^{k+1}_{s,t}-A^{k}_{s,t}\|_{L_{m}}\leqslant\Gamma_{3}(t-s)+2^{-k\varepsilon_{4}}\Gamma_{4}(t-s)^{1+\varepsilon_{4}}+2^{-k\varepsilon_{2}+2}\kappa_{m}\Gamma_{2}(t-s)^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon_{2}}\,.

(4.22)

However, recalling (
4.18
), we still have

\|I^{k}_{s,t}\|_{L_{m}}\leqslant 2^{-k\varepsilon_{1}+1}\Gamma_{1}(t-s)^{1+\varepsilon_{1}},

which together with (
4.21
) provides an alternative bound for
\|A^{k+1}_{s,t}-A^{k}_{s,t}\|_{L_{m}}
. Namely,

\|A^{k+1}_{s,t}-A^{k}_{s,t}\|_{L_{m}}\leqslant 2^{-k\varepsilon_{1}+1}\Gamma_{1}(t-s)^{1+\varepsilon_{1}}+2^{-k\varepsilon_{2}+2}\kappa_{m}\Gamma_{2}(t-s)^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon_{2}}\,.

(4.23)

Combining (
4.22
) and (
4.23
) together, we get that there exists a constant
C=C(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{4},m)
such that for any fixed integers
0\leqslant k\leqslant N

\displaystyle\|A_{s,t}^{N}-A_{s,t}\|_{L_{m}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{i=0}^{k}\|A_{s,t}^{i+1}-A_{s,t}^{i}\|_{L_{m}}+\sum_{i=k+1}^{N-1}\|A_{s,t}^{i+1}-A_{s,t}^{i}\|_{L_{m}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C(k\Gamma_{3}+2^{-k\varepsilon_{1}}\Gamma_{1}T^{\varepsilon_{1}})(t-s)+C\Gamma_{4}(t-s)^{1+\varepsilon_{4}}+C\Gamma_{2}(t-s)^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon_{2}},

where in the first sum we have applied (
4.22
) and in the second sum we used (
4.23
). Similar to the proof of
4.1
, we pass now to the limit
as
N\to\infty
(note that
k
remains fixed) with the help of Fatou’s lemma and (
4.4
). We deduce

\|\mathcal{A}_{t}-\mathcal{A}_{s}-A_{s,t}\|_{L_{m}}\leqslant C(k\Gamma_{3}+2^{-k\varepsilon_{1}}\Gamma_{1}T^{\varepsilon_{1}})(t-s)+C\Gamma_{4}(t-s)^{1+\varepsilon_{4}}+C\Gamma_{2}(t-s)^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon_{2}}.

Now, let us fine–tune the parameter
k
. If
\Gamma_{3}\geqslant\Gamma_{1}T^{\varepsilon_{1}}
, we choose
k=1
and the previous inequality implies (
4.12
). If
\Gamma_{3}<\Gamma_{1}T^{\varepsilon_{1}}
, we can choose
k\geqslant 1
so that
2^{-k\varepsilon_{1}}\Gamma_{1}T^{\varepsilon_{1}}\leqslant\Gamma_{3}\leqslant 2^{(1-k)\varepsilon_{1}}\Gamma_{1}T^{\varepsilon_{1}}
which optimizes the right-hand side above and contributes the logarithmic factor. This gives (
4.12
). ∎

Proof of
4.7
.

We use a slightly different version of (
4.16
)

\displaystyle A^{k+1}_{s,t}-A^{k}_{s,t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{2^{k}-1}\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{u^{k}_{i}}\delta A_{t^{k}_{i},u^{k}_{i},t^{k}_{i+1}}+\sum_{i=0}^{2^{k}-1}\bigl(\delta A_{t^{k}_{i},u^{k}_{i},t^{k}_{i+1}}-\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{u^{k}_{i}}\delta A_{t^{k}_{i},u^{k}_{i},t^{k}_{i+1}}\bigr)

\displaystyle=:\tilde{I}^{k}_{s,t}+\tilde{J}^{k}_{s,t}.

(4.24)

By (
4.13
),

|\tilde{I}^{k}_{s,t}|\leqslant\Gamma_{1}|t-s|^{\alpha_{1}}2^{-k\alpha_{1}}\sum_{i=0}^{2^{k}-1}\lambda(t^{k}_{i},t^{k}_{i+1})^{\beta_{1}}\leqslant\Gamma_{1}|t-s|^{\alpha_{1}}2^{-k(\alpha_{1}+\beta_{1}-1)}\lambda(s,t)^{\beta_{1}},

(4.25)

where the last inequality follows from the Hölder inequality and superadditivity of the random control
\lambda
. Note that
\tilde{J}^{k}_{s,t}
is the sum of martingale differences, hence, can be estimated analogously to
J^{k}_{s,t}
as in the proof of
4.1
. Applying the BDG and Minkowski inequalities, we have

\displaystyle\|\tilde{J}^{k}_{s,t}\|_{L_{m}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\kappa_{m}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{2^{k}-1}\|\delta A_{t^{k}_{i},u^{k}_{i},t^{k}_{i+1}}\|_{L_{m}}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Using condition (
4.3
) (with
n=m
and
\alpha_{2}=\beta_{2}=0
), we have

\displaystyle\|\tilde{J}^{k}_{s,t}\|_{L_{m}}\leqslant 2^{-k\varepsilon_{2}}8\kappa_{m}\Gamma_{2}|t-s|^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon_{2}}.

(4.26)

Thus, we get from (
4.2
) and (
4.25
)

|A^{k+1}_{s,t}-A^{k}_{s,t}|\leqslant\Gamma_{1}|t-s|^{\alpha_{1}}2^{-k(\alpha_{1}+\beta_{1}-1)}\lambda(s,t)^{\beta_{1}}+|\tilde{J}^{k}_{s,t}|,\,\text{a.s.}

(4.27)

Define
B_{s,t}:=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}|\tilde{J}^{k}_{s,t}|
. Using (
4.26
) and triangle inequality, we see that
B
satisfies (
4.15
).

It follows from (
4.27
) that

|A^{k+1}_{s,t}-A_{s,t}|\leqslant\sum_{i=0}^{k}|A^{i+1}_{s,t}-A^{i}_{s,t}|\leqslant C\Gamma_{1}|t-s|^{\alpha_{1}}\lambda(s,t)^{\beta_{1}}+B_{s,t}.

Sending
k\to\infty
and using condition (
4.4
), we obtain (
4.14
).
∎

5
Proofs of key propositions

In this section, we present the proofs of the propositions from
Section

3
. The regularization estimates which are necessary for the proofs are summarized in
Lemmas

5.1
and
5.2
below. The proofs of these lemmas are presented in
Section

6.1
.
We recall (
3.5
) which defines the space
{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
f\in\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}
be a bounded function. Let
m\in[2,\infty)
,
p\in[m,\infty]
and
\gamma\in(-2,0)
.
There exists a constant
C=C(\gamma,m,p)
such that for any
0\leqslant s\leqslant t\leqslant T
and any
\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R})\otimes\mathcal{F}_{s}
-measurable function
\kappa\in{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}
one has

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\int_{s}^{t}\int_{D}p_{T-r}(x,y)f(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-s}\kappa(y))\,dydr\Bigr\|_{L_{m}}\leqslant C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}(t-s)^{1+\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}}.

(5.1)

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a bounded continuous function in
\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}
,
T>0
, and
\psi\colon[0,T]\times{D}\times\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
be a measurable function adapted to the filtration
\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}
.
Let
m\in[2,\infty)
,
n\in[m,\infty]
,
p\in[n,\infty]
and
\tau\in(0,1)
be fixed numbers. Let
\gamma\in(-2+1/p,0)
and assume that

\gamma-\frac{1}{p}+4\tau>1.

(5.2)

Then the following statements hold.

1.

There exists a constant
C=C(\gamma,p,\tau,m)>0
independent of
T
such that for any
S\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in{D}}\Bigl\|\Bigl\|\int_{S}^{T}\int_{D}p_{T-r}(x,y)f(V_{r}(y)+\psi_{r}(y))dydr\Bigr\|_{{L_{m}}|\mathcal{F}_{S}}\Bigr\|_{L_{n}}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}(T-S)^{1+\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}}+C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{\tau,0}L_{m,n}({[S,T]})}}(T-S)^{\frac{3}{4}+\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}+\tau}.

(5.3)

2.

Let
\delta\in(0,1)
. There exists a constant
C=C(\gamma,p,\tau,\delta,m)>0
independent of
T
such that
for any
S\in[0,T]
,
x_{1},x_{2}\in{D}

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\int_{S}^{T}\int_{D}(p_{T-r}(x_{1},y)-p_{T-r}(x_{2},y))f(V_{r}(y)+\psi_{r}(y))dydr\Bigr\|_{{L_{m}}}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\delta}((T-S)^{1-\frac{\delta}{2}+\frac{1}{4}(\gamma-\frac{1}{p})}+[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{\tau,0}L_{m}({[S,T]})}}(T-S)^{\frac{3}{4}-\frac{\delta}{2}+\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}+\tau}).

(5.4)

3.

Let
\delta\in(0,1)
and
\bar{T}>0
. There exists a constant
C=C(\gamma,p,\tau,\delta,m)>0
independent of
T,\bar{T}
such that
for any
S\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in{D}}\Bigl\|\int_{S}^{T}\int_{D}(p_{T+\bar{T}-r}(x,y)-p_{T-r}(x,y))f(V_{r}(y)+\psi_{r}(y))dydr\Bigr\|_{{L_{m}}}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\bar{T}^{\frac{\delta}{2}}((T-S)^{1-\frac{\delta}{2}+\frac{1}{4}(\gamma-\frac{1}{p})}+[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{\tau,0}L_{m}({[S,T]})}}(T-S)^{\frac{3}{4}-\frac{\delta}{2}+\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}+\tau}).

(5.5)

Remark 5.3
.

Estimate (
1
) is an analogue of the following estimate by Davie in
[
Dav07b
]
for Brownian motion

\Bigl\|\int_{s}^{t}[f(B_{r}+x_{1})-f(B_{r}+x_{2})]dr\Bigr\|_{L_{m}}\leqslant C_{m}\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|f(z)||t-s|^{\frac{1}{2}}|x_{1}-x_{2}|,

(5.6)

which holds for every
s\leqslant t
,
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and bounded measurable
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Noting that the map
x\mapsto(f(x+x_{1})-f(x+x_{2}))/(|x_{1}-x_{2}|)
has finite
\mathcal{B}^{-1}_{\infty}
-norm and therefore (
5.6
) is indeed an estimate with distributions.
A closely related estimate is of the type

\Bigl\|\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{s}\bigl(\int_{s}^{t}f(X_{r})dr\bigr)\Bigr\|_{L_{\infty}}\leqslant C\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R})}|t-s|^{\theta},

(5.7)

where
p\geqslant d+1
,
\theta=\theta(p)=1-\frac{d}{2p}
,
X
is a martingale of the form
X_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{r}dB_{r}
,
\sigma
is adapted,
\Lambda^{-1}\geqslant|\sigma_{r}|\geqslant\Lambda
for all
r
,
\Lambda
is a deterministic positive constant. Estimate (
5.7
) follows from a general result of Krylov in
[
Kry87
]
and an argument similar to
[
GM01
, Corollary 3.2]
. When
f
is a non-negative function, by expanding moment and successively conditioning, one can obtain from the above estimate that for every integer
m\geqslant 2
,
s\leqslant t
,

\displaystyle\|\int_{s}^{t}f(X_{r})dr\|_{L_{m}}\leqslant C_{m}\|f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R})}|t-s|^{\theta},

(5.8)

which is comparable to Davie-type estimate (
5.6
).
However, the fact that
f
is a non-negative function is crucial and in particular, one cannot obtain (
5.6
) from such an argument.

As observed in
[
Lê20
]
for the case of fractional Brownian motion, one can indeed obtain estimates of the types (
5.6
) and (
5.8
) from estimates of the type (
5.7
) by mean of the stochastic sewing lemma for
f
being a distribution provided that
\theta>1/2
. This passage is also visible in the proof of
5.2
in
Section

6
.

5.1
Proof of
Propositions

3.2
,
3.3
and
3.4

Proof of
Proposition

3.2
.

We will use the estimate (
1
) from
5.2
.
Fix
m
,
f
,
u_{0}
satisfying the assumptions of the proposition.
Define for brevity

\psi(t,x):=u^{\eta;f}(t,x)-V(t,x),\quad t\in[0,T_{0}],\,\,x\in D.

First we note that since the function
f
is bounded, we have for any
(s,t)\in\Delta_{0,T_{0}}
,

\displaystyle|\psi_{t}(x)-P_{t-s}\psi_{s}(x)|=\Bigl|\int_{s}^{t}\int_{D}p_{t-r}(x,y)f(u^{\eta;f}_{r}(y))\,dydr\Bigr|\leqslant\sup_{z\in{D}}|f(z)||t-s|,

which implies that
[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{\tau,0}L_{m,\infty}({[0,T_{0}]})}}
is finite for any
\tau\in(0,1]
.

Let us apply now
5.2
with the following set of parameters:
\gamma=\beta-1/q
,
n=p=\infty
and
\tau=1+\beta/4-1/(4q)
. In other words, we consider
f
as a distribution
\mathcal{B}^{\beta-1/q}_{\infty}
, thanks to the embedding
\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{B}^{\beta-1/q}_{\infty}
(see (
3.6
)). Since
\beta-1/q>-3/2
, we see that (
5.2
) holds. Therefore, all the assumptions of
5.2
are satisfied and we obtain from (
1
) that there exists
C>0
such that for any
(s,t)\in\Delta_{0,T_{0}}

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in{D}}\|\|\psi_{t}(x)-P_{t-s}\psi_{s}(x)\|_{L_{m}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}\|_{L_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leqslant C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}(t-s)^{1+\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q}}\bigl(1+[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{1+\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q},0}L_{m,\infty}({[s,t]})}}(t-s)^{\frac{3}{4}+\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q}}\bigr),

(5.9)

where we used the fact that
\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta-1/q}_{\infty}}\leqslant C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}
thanks to (
3.6
). Fix now
S\in[0,T_{0}]
and
\ell\in(0,T_{0}-S]
. By dividing both sides of inequality (
5.1
) by
(t-s)^{1+\beta/4-1/(4q)}
and taking
supremum over all
(s,t)\in\Delta_{S,S+\ell}
, we get

[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{1+\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q},0}L_{m,\infty}({[S,S+\ell]})}}\leqslant C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}+C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{1+\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q},0}L_{m,\infty}({[S,S+\ell]})}}\ell^{\frac{3}{4}+\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q}}.

Note that
3/4+\beta/4-1/(4q)>0
(since
\beta-1/q>-3/2
) and the constant
C
does not depend on
S
or
\ell
. Hence, by choosing
\ell_{0}\in(0,T_{0})
small enough such that

C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}\ell_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}+\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q}}\leqslant\frac{1}{2},

we get that there exists
C_{1}>0
such that for any
S\in[0,T_{0}-\ell_{0}]

[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{1+\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q},0}L_{m,\infty}({[S,S+\ell_{0}]})}}\leqslant C_{1}\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}.

(5.10)

It remains to show that the above estimate implies (
3.7
). For arbitrary
(s,t)\in\Delta_{0,T_{0}}
, we choose
N\geqslant 2
such that
T_{0}/N\leqslant\ell_{0}<T_{0}/(N-1)
and define successively
s_{0}=s
,
s_{k}=s_{k-1}+(t-s)/N
for each
k=1,\dots,N
.
From the following telescopic identities

\displaystyle\psi_{t}-P_{t-s}\psi_{s}=\sum_{k=1}^{N}(P_{t-s_{k}}\psi_{s_{k}}-P_{t-s_{k-1}}\psi_{s_{k-1}})=\sum_{k=1}^{N}P_{t-s_{k}}(\psi_{s_{k}}-P_{s_{k}-s_{k-1}}\psi_{s_{k-1}}),

we apply triangle inequality and (
B.8
) to obtain that

\displaystyle\|\|\psi_{t}(x)-P_{t-s}\psi_{s}(x)\|_{L_{m}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}\|_{L_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{k=1}^{N}\|\|P_{t-s_{k}}(\psi_{s_{k}}-P_{s_{k}-s_{k-1}}\psi_{s_{k-1}})(x)\|_{L_{m}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}\|_{L_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{k=1}^{N}\sup_{y\in{D}}\|\|\psi_{s_{k}}(y)-P_{s_{k}-s_{k-1}}\psi_{s_{k-1}}(y)\|_{L_{m}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}\|_{L_{\infty}}.

(5.11)

Since
\mathcal{F}_{s}\subset\mathcal{F}_{s_{k-1}}
, one has

\|\psi_{s_{k}}(y)-P_{s_{k}-s_{k-1}}\psi_{s_{k-1}}(y)\|_{L_{m}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}=\|\|\psi_{s_{k}}(y)-P_{s_{k}-s_{k-1}}\psi_{s_{k-1}}(y)\|_{L_{m}|\mathcal{F}_{s_{k-1}}}\|_{L_{m}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}.

Note that
s_{k}-s_{k-1}\leqslant\ell_{0}
, we can apply (
5.10
) to obtain that for any
y\in{D}

\displaystyle\|\psi_{s_{k}}(y)-P_{s_{k}-s_{k-1}}\psi_{s_{k-1}}(y)\|_{L_{m}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{1}\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}(s_{k}-s_{k-1})^{1+\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q}}.

Hence we can continue (
5.1
) in the following way

\displaystyle\|\|\psi_{t}(x)-P_{t-s}\psi_{s}(x)\|_{L_{m}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}\|_{L_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{1}\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}\sum_{k=1}^{N}(s_{k}-s_{k-1})^{1+\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{1}\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}N^{1-\tau}(t-s)^{1+\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q}}.

Since
N<1+T_{0}/\ell_{0}
, this implies (
3.7
).
∎

To prove
Propositions

3.3
and
3.4
we will need the following statement. For a bounded continuous function
h:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
and a measurable function
\sigma\colon(0,T_{0}]\times{D}\times\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
define

K^{h;\sigma}(t,x):=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{D}p_{t-r}(x,y)h(\sigma(r,y))dydr,\quad t\in[0,T_{0}],\,\,x\in D.

(5.12)

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
\beta\leqslant 0
,
q\in[2,\infty]
. Assume that
\beta-1/q>-3/2
.
Let
h,f
be bounded continuous functions in
\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{q}
,
\eta\in{\mathbf{B}}(D)
. Let
u=u^{\eta;f}
be the solution to
Eq(
\eta;f
)
. Let
\delta\in(0,1)
. Then there exists a random variable
H
such that for a.s
\omega
, for any
x_{1},x_{2}\in{D}
,
s,t\in[0,T_{0}]

|K^{h;u}(t,x_{1})-K^{h;u}(s,x_{2})|\leqslant H(\omega)(1+|x_{1}|+|x_{2}|)(|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\delta}+|t-s|^{\frac{\delta}{2}}),

(5.13)

and
\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057ptH\leqslant C\|h\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}(1+\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}})
, where the constant
C=C(\beta,q,\delta,\nu,T_{0})>0
is independent of
\eta
,
h
,
f
.

Proof.

In the proof, for brevity, we write
u=u^{\eta;f}
,
K=K^{h;u}
.
Fix
\delta\in(0,1)
,
m\in[2,\infty)
. Let
\delta^{\prime}\in(\delta,1)
.
Note that for
x_{1},x_{2}\in{D}
and
(s,t)\in\Delta_{0,T_{0}}
we have

\displaystyle\|K(t,x_{1})-K(s,x_{2})\|_{{L_{m}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|K(t,x_{1})-K(t,x_{2})\|_{{L_{m}}}+\|K(t,x_{2})-P_{t-s}K(s,x_{2})\|_{{L_{m}}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=:}+\|(P_{t-s}-Id)K(s,x_{2})\|_{{L_{m}}}

\displaystyle=:I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}.

(5.14)

To bound
I_{1}
,
I_{2}
, and
I_{3}
we will use
5.2
with the following parameters:

\psi=u-V,\,\gamma=\beta-\frac{1}{q},\,n=m,\,p=\infty,\,\tau=1+\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q},\,T=t.

(5.15)

First, the estimate in
Proposition

3.2
and
(
3.4
) give

[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{1+\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q},0}L_{m}({[0,T_{0}]})}}\leqslant[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{1+\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q},0}L_{m,\infty}({[0,T_{0}]})}}\leqslant C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}.

(5.16)

Second,
3.1
implies that (
5.2
) holds. Therefore, all the conditions of
5.2
are satisfied.
By part (ii) of the lemma with
S=0
, we get

I_{1}\leqslant C(T_{0})\|h\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}(1+\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}})|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\delta^{\prime}},

(5.17)

where we also used the bound (
5.16
).

To bound the second term in (
5.1
),
I_{2}
, we use
5.2
(i) with the parameters described in (
5.15
) as well as
n=m
,
x=x_{2}
,
S=s
. We get

I_{2}=\Bigl\|\int_{s}^{t}\int_{D}p_{t-r}(x_{2},y)h(u(r,y))dydr\Bigr\|_{{L_{m}}}\leqslant C(T_{0})\|h\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}(1+\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}})(t-s)^{1+\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q}},

(5.18)

where we used again bound (
5.16
).

Finally, let us bound
I_{3}
. We note that

\displaystyle I_{3}=\Bigl\|\int_{0}^{s}\int_{D}[p_{t-r}(x,y)-p_{s-r}(x,y)]h(u_{r}(y))dydr\Bigr\|_{L_{m}}.

In view of (
5.16
), we can apply
5.2
(iii) to obtain

\displaystyle I_{3}\leqslant C(T_{0})\|h\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}(1+\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}})(t-s)^{\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2}}.

Now combining this with (
5.17
), (
5.18
) and
substituting into (
5.1
), we arrive at

\|K(t,x_{1})-K(s,x_{2})\|_{{L_{m}}}\leqslant C(T_{0})R(|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\delta^{\prime}}+|t-s|^{\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2}}),

where we used the fact that
1+\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q}>\frac{1}{2}>\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2}
and denoted

R:=\|h\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}(1+\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}})
.

Recall that
\delta\in(0,\delta^{\prime})
and
m
is arbitrarily large. Then, by the Kolmogorov continuity theorem (which is an easy extension of
[
Kun90
, Theorem 1.4.1]
), there exists a random variable
H(\omega)
such that for any
\omega\in\Omega
,

x_{1},x_{2}\in{D}
,
s,t\in[0,T_{0}]

we have

|K(t,x_{1})-K(s,x_{2})|\leqslant H(\omega)(1+|x_{1}|+|x_{2}|)(|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\delta}+|t-s|^{\frac{\delta}{2}}),

and
\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057ptH(\omega)\leqslant C(T_{0})R
.
This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

3.3
.

Fix the sequences
(b_{n}^{\prime})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
,
(b_{n}^{\prime\prime})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
,
(u_{0,n}^{\prime})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
,
(u_{0,n}^{\prime\prime})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
.
Recall definition (
5.12
). Put

K_{n}^{\prime}:=K^{b_{n}^{\prime};u_{n}^{\prime}},\,\,K_{n}^{\prime}:=K^{b_{n}^{\prime\prime};u_{n}^{\prime\prime}}.

Note that

\widetilde{u}_{n}^{\prime}(t)=K_{n}^{\prime}(t)+V(t),\quad\widetilde{u}_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)=K_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)+V(t).

(5.19)

For
M>0
,
\delta\in(0,1)
,
\nu>0
put

\displaystyle A_{M}:=

\displaystyle\big\{f\in\mathcal{C}([0,T_{0}]\times{D}):f(0,\cdot)=0\textrm{ and for every }x_{1},x_{2}\in{D},s,t\in[0,T_{0}]

\displaystyle\quad\quad|f_{t}(x_{1})-f_{s}(x_{2})|\leqslant M(1+|x_{1}|+|x_{2}|)(|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\delta}+|t-s|^{\frac{\delta}{2}})\big\}.

By the Arzela–Ascoli theorem, for each
M>0
the set
A_{M}
is a compact set in the space
\mathcal{C}_{\textrm{uc}}([0,T_{0}]\times{D})
.
We apply
5.4
with
h=f=b_{n}^{\prime}
,
\eta=u^{\prime}_{0,n}
. For any
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, we derive from (
5.13
) and
the Chebyshev inequality

\mathsf{P}(K^{\prime}_{n}\notin A_{M})\leqslant C\|b_{n}^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}(1+\|b_{n}^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}})M^{-1}\leqslant CRM^{-1},

where we denoted
R:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\|b_{n}^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}(1+\|b_{n}^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}})<\infty
. Thus, the sequence
(K_{n}^{\prime})_{n\geqslant 0}
is tight in
\mathcal{C}_{\textrm{uc}}([0,T_{0}]\times{D})
. Similarly,
(K_{n}^{\prime\prime})_{n\geqslant 0}
is tight in
\mathcal{C}_{\textrm{uc}}([0,T_{0}]\times{D})
and obviously the constant sequence
(V)_{n\geqslant 0}
is tight in this space as well. Hence
the sequence
(K_{n}^{\prime},K_{n}^{\prime\prime},V)_{n\geqslant 0}
is tight in
[\mathcal{C}_{\textrm{uc}}([0,T_{0}]\times{D})]^{3}
.
Since this space is separable, by the Prokhorov theorem there exists
a subsequence

(n_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
such that
(K_{n_{k}}^{\prime},K_{n_{k}}^{\prime\prime},V)_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
converges weakly in the space
[\mathcal{C}([0,T_{0}]\times{D})]^{3}
.
Recalling (
5.19
), we see that
(\widetilde{u}_{n_{k}}^{\prime},\widetilde{u}_{n_{k}}^{\prime\prime},V)_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
converges weakly. This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

3.4
.

Step 1.
We show that
u
is a solution to (
1.1
). We define for each
(t,x)\in[0,T_{0}]\times{D}

K(t,x):=\widetilde{u}(t,x)-V(t,x).

(5.20)

Let
(\bar{b}^{n})
be an arbitrary sequence of functions in
\mathcal{C}_{b}^{\infty}
converging to
b
in
\mathcal{B}^{\beta-}_{q}
. Fix arbitrary
R>0
.
In view of
2.3
, we need to show that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in{D}\\
|x|\leqslant R\end{subarray}}|K^{\bar{b}^{n};u}_{t}(x)-K_{t}(x)|=0\quad\textrm{in probability}.

(5.21)

By triangle inequality, we decompose for any
k,n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
x\in{D}
,
t\in[0,T_{0}]

\displaystyle|K^{\bar{b}^{n};u}_{t}(x)-K_{t}(x)|

\displaystyle\leqslant|K^{\bar{b}^{n};u}_{t}(x)-K^{\bar{b}^{n};u^{k}}_{t}(x)|+|K^{\bar{b}^{n};u^{k}}_{t}(x)-K^{b^{k};u^{k}}_{t}(x)|

\displaystyle\phantom{le}+|K^{b^{k};u^{k}}_{t}(x)-K_{t}(x)|

\displaystyle=:I_{1}(n,k,t,x)+I_{2}(n,k,t,x)+I_{3}(k,t,x).

(5.22)

Let us estimate successively all the terms in the right-hand side of (
5.1
).
Since for any fixed
n
the function
\bar{b}^{n}
is a smooth bounded function, we have for any
|x|\leqslant R
,
x\in{D}
,
M>R

\displaystyle I_{1}(n,k,t,x)

\displaystyle=\Bigl|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{D}p_{t-r}(x,y)\bigl(\bar{b}^{n}(u_{r}(y))-\bar{b}^{n}(u_{r}^{k}(y))\bigr)\,dydr\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\bar{b}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{D}\cap\{|y|\leqslant M\}}p_{t-r}(x,y)|u_{r}(y)-u_{r}^{k}(y)|\,dydr

\displaystyle\phantom{\leqslant}+2\|\bar{b}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{D}\cap\{|y|\geqslant M\}}p_{t-r}(x,y)\,dydr

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\bar{b}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}T_{0}\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in{D}\\
|y|\leqslant M\end{subarray}}|u_{r}(y)-u_{r}^{k}(y)|+C\|\bar{b}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}}T_{0}e^{-\frac{(M-R)^{2}}{T_{0}}}.

We use triangle inequality and the estimate
|P_{t}\varphi(x)|\leqslant\sup_{y\in{D}}|\varphi(y)|
valid for any bounded measurable function
\varphi
to obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in{D}\\
|y|\leqslant M\end{subarray}}|u_{r}(y)-u_{r}^{k}(y)|\leqslant\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in{D}\\
|y|\leqslant M\end{subarray}}|\widetilde{u}_{r}(y)-\widetilde{u}_{r}^{k}(y)|+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in{D}\end{subarray}}|u_{0}(y)-u_{0}^{k}(y)|.

By assumption, the above implies that
u^{k}
converges to
u
in
\mathcal{C}_{\textrm{uc}}([0,T_{0}]\times{D})
in probability. Hence, in the previous estimates for
I_{1}
, we send
k\to\infty
then
M\to\infty
to see that

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in{D}\\
|x|\leqslant R\end{subarray}}I_{1}(n,k,t,x)=0\quad\textrm{in probability}.

(5.23)

To bound
I_{2}
we fix
\beta^{\prime}<\beta
such that
\beta^{\prime}-1/q>-3/2
. This is possible thanks to
3.1
. We apply
5.4
with
h=\bar{b}^{n}-b^{k}
,
f=b^{k}
,
\eta=u_{0}^{k}
,
x_{1}=x_{2}=x
,
s=0
,
\beta^{\prime}
in place of
\beta
. We get that there exists a random variable
H_{n,k}
such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in{D}\\
|x|\leqslant R\end{subarray}}I_{2}(n,k,t,x)=\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in{D}\\
|x|\leqslant R\end{subarray}}|K^{\bar{b}^{n}-b^{k};u^{k}}_{t}(x)|\leqslant H_{n,k}(\omega)(1+R)(1+T_{0})

and

\displaystyle\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057ptH_{n,k}(\omega)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|\bar{b}^{n}-b^{k}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta^{\prime}}_{q}}(1+\|b^{k}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta^{\prime}}_{q}})

\displaystyle\leqslant C(\|\bar{b}^{n}-b\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta^{\prime}}_{q}}+\|b^{k}-b\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta^{\prime}}_{q}})(1+\sup_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\|b^{r}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta^{\prime}}_{q}}),

where again the constant
C
does not depend on
n
,
k
. Thus for any
\varepsilon>0
one has

\mathsf{P}(\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in{D}\\
|x|\leqslant R\end{subarray}}I_{2}(n,k,t,x)>\varepsilon)\\
\leqslant C\varepsilon^{-1}(\|\bar{b}^{n}-b\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta^{\prime}}_{q}}+\|b^{k}-b\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta^{\prime}}_{q}})(1+\sup_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\|b^{r}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta^{\prime}}_{q}})(1+R)(1+T_{0}).

This implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in{D}\\
|x|\leqslant R\end{subarray}}I_{2}(n,k,t,x)=0\quad\textrm{in probability}.

To treat
I_{3}
, we first derive from (
5.12
) and the definition of
u^{k}
that for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
\widetilde{u}^{k}=K^{b^{k};u^{k}}+V^{k}
.
Hence, together with (
5.20
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in{D}\\
|x|\leqslant R\end{subarray}}I_{3}(k,t,x)

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in{D}\\
|x|\leqslant R\end{subarray}}|K^{b^{k};u^{k}}_{t}(x)-K_{t}(x)|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in{D}\\
|x|\leqslant R\end{subarray}}(|\widetilde{u}_{t}^{k}(x)-\widetilde{u}_{t}(x)|+|V_{t}^{k}(x)-V_{t}(x)|).

This implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in{D}\\
|x|\leqslant R\end{subarray}}I_{3}(k,t,x)=0\quad\textrm{in probability}.

(5.24)

Finally, combining (
5.1
)-(
5.24
) and (
5.1
), we obtain (
5.21
).

Step 2.
It remains to show (
3.8
). It follows from
Proposition

3.2
, that there exists a constant
C
such that for every
(s,t)\in\Delta_{0,T_{0}}
,
x\in{D}
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}

\displaystyle\|u^{n}_{t}(x)-V^{n}_{t}(x)-P_{t-s}(u^{n}_{s}(x)-V^{n}_{s}(x))\|_{L_{m}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}\leqslant C(t-s)^{1-\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q}}.

Note that we used here that
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\|b^{n}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}<\infty

thanks to the definition of convergence in
\mathcal{B}^{\beta-}_{q}
. It follows from the mild formulation of
u^{n}
(recall that
u^{n}
solves

Eq(
u^{n}_{0};b^{n}
)
with
V^{n}
in place of
V
),

\displaystyle u^{n}_{t}(x)-V^{n}_{t}(x)-P_{t-s}(u^{n}_{s}(x)-V^{n}_{s}(x))=K^{b^{n};u^{n}}_{t}(x)-P_{t-s}K^{b^{n};u^{n}}_{s}(x).

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\|K^{b^{n};u^{n}}_{t}(x)-P_{t-s}K^{b^{n};u^{n}}_{s}(x)\|_{L_{m}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}\leqslant C(t-s)^{1-\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q}}.

(5.25)

Putting
s=0
, the previous estimate implies that

\displaystyle\|K^{b^{n};u^{n}}_{t}(x)\|_{L_{m}}\leqslant Ct^{1-\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q}}.

(5.26)

On the other hand, we see from (
5.24
) that
\lim_{n\to\infty}K^{b^{n};u^{n}}=K
in
\mathcal{C}_{\textrm{uc}}([0,T_{0}]\times{D})
in probability. Hence, by passing to the limit as
n\to\infty
in (
5.26
) and applying Fatou’s lemma, we see that
\sup_{(t,x)\in[0,T_{0}]\times{D}}\|K_{t}(x)\|_{L_{m}}<\infty
. By
B.4
, we see that
P_{t-s}K_{s}(x)
is well-defined as an
L_{m}
-integrable random variable.
Furthermore, in view of (
5.26
) and the convergence of
K^{b^{n};u^{n}}
to
K
, we obtain from
Lemma

B.5
that for each fixed
0\leqslant s\leqslant t\leqslant T_{0}
,
x\in{D}

\lim_{n\to\infty}P_{t-s}K^{b^{n};u^{n}}_{s}(x)=P_{t-s}K_{s}(x)\quad\textrm{in probability}.

Therefore, we can pass to the limit as
n\to\infty
in (
5.25
) and apply Fatou’s lemma to obtain that

\|u_{t}(x)-V_{t}(x)-P_{t-s}(u_{s}(x)-V_{s}(x))\|_{L_{m}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}=\|K_{t}(x)-P_{t-s}K_{s}(x)\|_{L_{m}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}\leqslant C(t-s)^{1-\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q}},

where we also used the definition of
K
in (
5.20
). This implies (
3.8
).
∎

5.2
Proof of
Proposition

3.6

In this subsection we will use the following additional notation. Let
(S,T)\in\Delta_{0,T_{0}}
. For a measurable function
Z\colon\Delta_{S,T}\times{D}\times\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
,
\tau\in[0,1]
,
m\geqslant 1
we put

\|Z\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{\tau,0}L_{m}({[S,T]})}}:=\sup_{(s,t)\in\Delta_{S,T}}\sup_{x\in{D}}\frac{\|Z_{s,t}(x)\|_{{L_{m}}}}{|t-s|^{\tau}}.

(5.27)

Till the end of the subsection fix the parameters
\beta
,
q
satisfying the conditions of
Proposition

3.6
and
b\in\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}
. We fix also
(u_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{0}]},(v_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\in\mathcal{V}(3/4)
, which are as in the statement of
Proposition

3.6
.

We define

\psi_{t}:=u_{t}-V_{t},\,\,\varphi_{t}:=v_{t}-V_{t},\,\,z_{t}:=u_{t}-v_{t}=\psi_{t}-\varphi_{t},\quad t\in[0,T_{0}].

Our goal is to prove that
z(t)=0
for all
t\in[0,T_{0}]
. Fix
m\in[2,\infty)
such that
m\leqslant q
.
We see that (
3.12
) and the fact that
u,v\in\mathcal{V}(3/4)
implies that

[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{3/4,0}L_{m,\infty}({[0,T_{0}]})}}<\infty,\,\,[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{3/4,0}L_{m}({[0,T_{0}]})}}<\infty,\,\,[\varphi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{3/4,0}L_{m}({[0,T_{0}]})}}<\infty.

This in turn yields that for any
t\in[0,T_{0}]
one has

\varphi_{t},\,\psi_{t}\in{\mathbf{B}L_{m}},

(5.28)

where the space
{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}
is introduced in (
3.5
).

Recall that the process
v
satisfies condition
(2′)
of
Remark

2.7
. We fix a sequence of smooth functions
(b_{n})
which appeared there. For
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
introduce the process

H^{n,\psi}_{s,t}(x):=\int_{s}^{t}\int_{D}p_{t-r}(x,y)b_{n}(V_{r}(y)+P_{t-r}\psi_{s}(y))\,dy\,dr,\quad(s,t)\in\Delta_{0,T_{0}},\,x\in{D}.

(5.29)

Define
H^{n,\varphi}_{s,t}(x)
in a similar way with
\varphi_{s}
in place of
\psi_{s}
in the right–hand side of (
5.29
). Note that the expressions
P_{t-r}\psi_{s}
and
P_{t-r}\varphi_{s}
are well-defined thanks to (
5.28
) and
B.4
.

Our first step in obtaining uniqueness is to pass to the limit as
n\to\infty
in (
5.29
).

Lemma 5.5
.

For each
x\in{D}
,
(s,t)\in\Delta_{0,T_{0}}
there exist random variables
H^{\psi}_{s,t}(x)
,
H^{\varphi}_{s,t}(x)
, such that

H^{n,\psi}_{s,t}(x)\to H^{\psi}_{s,t}(x),\,\,\,H^{n,\varphi}_{s,t}(x)\to H^{\varphi}_{s,t}(x),\qquad\text{in probability as $n\to\infty$}.

Furthermore, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
(s,t)\in\Delta_{0,T_{0}}
we have

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in{D}}\|H^{\psi}_{s,t}(x)-H^{\varphi}_{s,t}(x)\|_{{L_{m}}}\leqslant C(1+T_{0})\,\|z_{s}\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}(t-s)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(5.30)

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in{D}}\|H^{\psi}_{s,t}(x)-H^{\varphi}_{s,t}(x)\|_{{L_{m}}}\leqslant C(1+T_{0})\,(t-s)^{\frac{3}{4}}.

(5.31)

The lemma is proved using the stochastic sewing lemma. We postpone the proof till
Section

6.2
.

Recall the notation (
5.12
). Denote
K^{u}:=\psi-Pu_{0}
and
K^{v}:=\varphi-Pu_{0}
. It follows from
2.3
and condition
(2′)
of
Remark

2.7
, that
K^{b^{n};u}(t,x)\to K^{u}(t,x)
and
K^{b^{n};v}(t,x)\to K^{v}(t,x)
in probability as
n\to\infty
for any
t\in[0,T_{0}]
,
x\in{D}
.

Lemma 5.6
.

For every fixed
(s,t)\in\Delta_{0,T_{0}}
,
x\in{D}
we have

P_{t-s}K^{b_{n};u}_{s}(x)\to P_{t-s}K^{u}_{s}(x),\,\,\,\,P_{t-s}K^{b_{n};v}_{s}(x)\to P_{t-s}K^{v}_{s}(x)\,\,\,\,\text{ in probability as $n\to\infty$}.

It follows from
5.5
, that we can now define

R_{s,t}:=(\psi_{t}-P_{t-s}\psi_{s}-H^{\psi}_{s,t})-(\varphi_{t}-P_{t-s}\varphi_{s}-H^{\varphi}_{s,t}),\quad(s,t)\in\Delta_{0,T_{0}}.

(5.32)

The next result is crucial for proving that
z\equiv 0
and thus obtaining strong uniqueness.

Lemma 5.7
.

There exists
\delta=\delta(\beta,q)\in(0,1/2)
such that for any
\tau\in(1/2,1]
there exists a constant
C=C(T_{0},\|b\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}},[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{3/4,0}L_{m,\infty}({[0,T_{0}]})}},[\varphi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{3/4,0}L_{m}({[0,T_{0}]})}})
such that for any
(s,t)\in\Delta_{0,T_{0}}

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in{D}}\|z_{t}(x)-P_{t-s}z_{s}(x)\|_{L_{m}}\leqslant

\displaystyle C(\|z\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{0,0}L_{m}({[s,t]})}}+\|R\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{\tau,0}L_{m}({[s,t]})}})(t-s)^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}

\displaystyle+C\|z\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{0,0}L_{m}({[s,t]})}}|\log(\|z\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{0,0}L_{m}({[s,t]})}})|(t-s),

(5.33)

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in{D}}\|R_{s,t}(x)\|_{L_{m}}\leqslant

\displaystyle C(\|z\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{0,0}L_{m}({[s,t]})}}+\|R\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{\tau,0}L_{m}({[s,t]})}})(t-s)^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}

\displaystyle+C\|z\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{0,0}L_{m}({[s,t]})}}|\log(\|z\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{0,0}L_{m}({[s,t]})}})|(t-s).

(5.34)

The proof is presented in
Section

6.2
, in which we use the stochastic sewing lemma with critical exponent,
4.5
.

Now we are ready to prove the main result of this subsection: uniqueness of solutions of equation (
1.1
).

Proof of
Proposition

3.6
.

Step 1.
We show that there exist constants
\ell,C
such that

\|z_{t}-P_{t-s}z_{s}\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}\leqslant C\|z\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{0,0}L_{m}({[s,t]})}}(t-s)^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}+C\|z\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{0,0}L_{m}({[s,t]})}}|\log(\|z\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{0,0}L_{m}({[s,t]})}})|(t-s)

(5.35)

for any
(s,t)\in\Delta_{0,T_{0}}
satisfying
t-s\leqslant\ell
.

We first observe that
\|R\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{3/4,0}L_{m}({[0,T_{0}]})}}
is finite. Indeed, from (
5.32
) and (
5.31
) we see that

\displaystyle\|R\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{3/4,0}L_{m}({[0,T_{0}]})}}

\displaystyle\leqslant[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{3/4,0}L_{m}({[0,T_{0}]})}}+[\varphi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{3/4,0}L_{m}({[0,T_{0}]})}}+\|H^{\psi}-H^{\varphi}\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{3/4,0}L_{m}({[0,T_{0}]})}}

\displaystyle\leqslant[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{3/4,0}L_{m}({[0,T_{0}]})}}+[\varphi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{3/4,0}L_{m}({[0,T_{0}]})}}+C(1+T_{0})\|b\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}<\infty.

Fix now
(S,T)\in\Delta_{0,T_{0}}
. Let
\delta
be as in
5.7
. Let us apply
5.7
with
\tau:=1/2+\delta/2
.
Dividing both sides of (
5.34
) by
(t-s)^{1/2+\delta/2}
and taking supremum over
(s,t)\in\Delta_{S,T}
we deduce
for some constant
C_{1}:=C(\|b\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}},[\psi]_{\mathcal{C}^{3/4,0}L_{m,\infty}([0,T_{0}])},[\varphi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{3/4,0}L_{m}({[0,T_{0}]})}})
which does not depend on

T
,
S
that

\displaystyle\|R\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{1/2+\delta/2,0}L_{m}({[S,T]})}}\leqslant

\displaystyle C_{1}(\|z\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{0,0}L_{m}({[S,T]})}}+\|R\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{1/2+\delta/2,0}L_{m}({[S,T]})}})(T-S)^{\frac{\delta}{2}}

\displaystyle+C_{1}\|z\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{0,0}L_{m}({[S,T]})}}|\log(\|z\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{0,0}L_{m}({[S,T]})}})|(T-S)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\delta}{2}}.

(5.36)

Let
\ell=\ell(C_{1},\beta,q)<(1\wedge T_{0})
be such that

C_{1}\ell^{\frac{\delta}{2}}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}.

Then for any
(S,T)\in\Delta_{0,T_{0}}
such that
T-S\leqslant\ell
, we derive from (
5.2
) for
C_{2}:=2C_{1}
that

\|R\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{1/2+\delta/2,0}L_{m}({[S,T]})}}\leqslant C_{2}\|z\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{0,0}L_{m}({[S,T]})}}+C_{2}\|z\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{0,0}L_{m}({[S,T]})}}|\log(\|z\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{0,0}L_{m}({[S,T]})}})|(T-S)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\delta}{2}}.

In the above, we have used the fact that
1/2+\delta/2\leqslant 3/4
and hence
\|R\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{1/2+\delta/2,0}L_{m}({[S,T]})}}\leqslant\|R\|_{{\mathcal{C}^{3/4,0}L_{m}({[0,T_{0}]})}}
is finite.
Substituting this into (
5.7
), we obtain (
5.35
).

Step 2.
We show that the map
t\mapsto\|z_{t}\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}
is continuous on
[0,T_{0}]
.
By triangle inequality, we have for every
(s,t)\in\Delta_{0,T_{0}}
,

\displaystyle|\|z_{t}\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}-\|z_{s}\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}|\leqslant\|z_{t}-z_{s}\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}\leqslant\|z_{t}-P_{t-s}z_{s}\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}+\|P_{t-s}z_{s}-z_{s}\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}.

From (
5.35
), it is clear that
\lim_{t\downarrow s}\|z_{t}-P_{t-s}z_{s}\|_{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}=0
and
\lim_{s\uparrow t}\|z_{t}-P_{t-s}z_{s}\|_{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}=0
. It remains to consider the last term in the above estimate.

Since
u=Pu_{0}+K^{u}+V
and
v=Pu_{0}+K^{v}+V
by definition, we see that
z=K^{u}-K^{v}
. Hence, it suffices to show that
P_{t-s}K^{u}_{s}-K^{u}_{s}
and
P_{t-s}K^{v}_{s}-K^{v}_{s}
converge to
0
in
{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}
as
t\downarrow s
and
s\uparrow t
.
We have for each
x\in{D}
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,

P_{t-s}K^{b^{n};u}_{s}(x)-K^{b^{n};u}_{s}(x)=\int_{0}^{s}\int_{D}[p_{t-r}(x,y)-p_{s-r}(x,y)]b^{n}(u_{r}(y))dydr.

Fix arbitrary
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Let us apply
5.2
(iii) with
f=b^{n}
,
\gamma=\beta
,
p=q
,
S=0
,
T=s
,
\bar{T}=t-s
,
\delta=\varepsilon
,
\tau=3/4
. We see that condition (
5.2
) is satisfied and thus we obtain

\|P_{t-s}K^{b^{n};u}_{s}(x)-K^{b^{n};u}_{s}(x)\|_{L_{m}}\leqslant C(\varepsilon,T_{0},[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{3/4,0}L_{m}({[0,T_{0}]})}})\|b\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}(t-s)^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}},

where we also used the fact that
\|b_{n}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}\leqslant\|b\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}
.
Applying
Lemma

5.6
and Fatou’s lemma, we can pass to the limit as
n\to\infty
in the above inequality to obtain by Fatou’s lemma that

\displaystyle\|P_{t-s}K^{u}_{s}(x)-K^{u}_{s}(x)\|_{L_{m}}\leqslant C(\varepsilon,T_{0},[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{3/4,0}L_{m}({[0,T_{0}]})}})\|b\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}(t-s)^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}.

This implies that
\lim\|P_{t-s}K^{u}_{s}-K^{u}_{s}\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}=0
as
t\downarrow s
and
s\uparrow t
. The convergence of
P_{t-s}K^{v}_{s}-K^{v}_{s}
to
0
is obtained by exactly the same way.

Step 3.
We show by contradiction that
z\equiv 0
.
Suppose that
\|z_{t}\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}
is not identically
0
on
[0,T_{0}]
. Choose
k_{0}\geqslant 1
such that
2^{-k_{0}}<\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\|z_{t}\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}
. Then for each integer
k\geqslant k_{0}
, define

t_{k}=\inf\{t\in[0,T_{0}]:\|z_{t}\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}\geqslant 2^{-k}\}.

It is evident that each
t_{k}
is well defined. In addition,
\|z_{t}\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}<2^{-k}
for
t<t_{k}
while
\|z_{t_{k}}\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}=2^{-k}
by continuity shown in the previous step. Consequently, the sequence
\{t_{k}\}_{k\geqslant k_{0}}
is strictly decreasing. For
k
sufficiently large so that
t_{k}-t_{k+1}\leqslant\ell
, estimate (
5.35
) with
(s,t)=(t_{k+1},t_{k})
yields

\|z_{t_{k}}-P_{t_{k}-t_{k+1}}z_{t_{k+1}}\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}\leqslant C2^{-k}(t_{k}-t_{k+1})^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}+C2^{-k}k(t_{k}-t_{k+1}).

On the other hand, by (
B.8
),
\|P_{t_{k}-t_{k+1}}z_{t_{k+1}}\|_{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}\leqslant\|z_{t_{k+1}}\|_{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}=2^{-k-1}
and hence by triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\|z_{t_{k}}-P_{t_{k}-t_{k+1}}z_{t_{k+1}}\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}\geqslant\|z_{t_{k}}\|_{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}-\|P_{t_{k}-t_{k+1}}z_{t_{k+1}}\|_{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}\geqslant 2^{-k-1}.

It follows that

\displaystyle 2^{-k-1}\leqslant C2^{-k}(t_{k}-t_{k+1})^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}+C2^{-k}k(t_{k}-t_{k+1})

which implies that
t_{k}-t_{k+1}\geqslant\bar{C}(1+k)^{-1}
for some constant
\bar{C}
. This implies that
\sum_{k\geqslant k_{0}}(t_{k}-t_{k+1})=\infty
, which is a contradiction because
\{t_{k}\}
is a decreasing sequence in
[0,T_{0}]
. We conclude that
z\equiv 0
, and hence,
u=v
.
∎

5.3
Proof of
Proposition

3.8

Let
u
be a solution to (
1.1
) and
m
be arbitrary in
[2,\infty)
. Since
b
is a non-negative measure, we can choose a sequence of smooth bounded non-negative functions
(b^{n})
which converges to
b
in
\mathcal{B}^{0-}_{1}
and
\|b^{n}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{0}_{1}}\leqslant\|b\|_{\mathcal{B}^{0}_{1}}
(see
Lemma

A.3
). For each
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
t\in[0,T_{0}]
,
x\in{D}
, define

K^{n}_{t}(x)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{D}p_{t-r}(x,y)b^{n}(u_{r}(y))dydr.

By
2.3
, we see that
K^{n}
converges to
K
in
\mathcal{C}_{\textrm{uc}}([0,T_{0}]\times{D})
in probability. By passing through a subsequence, we can assume without loss of generality that this convergence is almost sure. Hence, we can find
\Omega^{*}\subset\Omega
such that
\mathsf{P}(\Omega^{*})=1
and that
K^{n}(\omega)
converges to
K(\omega)
in
\mathcal{C}_{\textrm{uc}}([0,T_{0}]\times{D})
for every
\omega\in\Omega^{*}
. Since
(t,x,\omega)\mapsto K^{n}_{t}(x,\omega)
is non-negative measurable, so is
(t,x,\omega)\mapsto K_{t}(x,\omega)
. As a consequence
(T,t,x,\omega)\mapsto P_{T-t}K_{t}(x,\omega)
is well-defined as a non-negative measurable function. We note that at this stage, we do not rule out the possibility that
P_{T-t}K_{t}(x,\omega)
may take infinite value. We divide the proof into several steps.

Step 1.
Fix arbitrary
\omega\in\Omega^{*}
. We show that

\displaystyle P_{T-t}K_{t}(x,\omega)\geqslant P_{T-s}K_{s}(x,\omega)\textrm{ for every }0\leqslant s\leqslant t\leqslant T\leqslant T_{0}\textrm{ and }x\in{D}.

(5.37)

For simplicity, we omit the dependence on
\omega
. By definition, we have

\displaystyle P_{T-t}K^{n}_{t}(x)-P_{T-s}K^{n}_{s}(x)=\int_{s}^{t}\int_{D}p_{T-r}(x,y)b^{n}(u_{r}(y))dydr

which implies that
P_{T-t}K^{n}_{t}(x)\geqslant P_{T-s}K^{n}_{s}(x)
for every
0\leqslant s\leqslant t\leqslant T\leqslant T_{0}
and
x\in{D}
. In particular, setting
T=t
, one gets
P_{t-s}K^{n}_{s}(x)\leqslant K^{n}_{t}(x)
. Applying this inequality and Fatou’s lemma, we have

\displaystyle P_{t-s}K_{s}(x)\leqslant\liminf_{n}P_{t-s}K^{n}_{s}(x)\leqslant\liminf_{n}K^{n}_{t}(x)=K_{t}(x).

This shows that
P_{t-s}K_{s}(x)\leqslant K_{t}(x)
. Applying
P_{T-t}
on both sides, we obtain (
5.37
).

Step 2.

Define
\psi=u-V=K+Pu_{0}
and

A^{T,n}_{s,t}(x):=\int_{s}^{t}\int_{D}p_{T-r}(x,y)b^{n}(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-s}\psi_{s}(y))\,dydr,\,\,\,0\leqslant s\leqslant t\leqslant T\leqslant T_{0},\,x\in{D}.

We claim that

\|A^{T,n}_{s,t}(x)\|_{L_{m}}\leqslant C\|b\|_{\mathcal{B}^{0}_{1}}|t-s|^{3/4}.

(5.38)

Indeed, for each integer
j\geqslant 1
, define
\psi^{j}_{t}(x):=(K_{t}(x)\wedge j)+P_{t}u_{0}(x)
which belongs to
{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}
.
We note that measures belong to
\mathcal{B}^{0}_{1}
, which is embedded in
\mathcal{B}^{1/m-1}_{m}
(see (
3.6
)). Applying
Lemma

5.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\int_{s}^{t}\int_{D}p_{T-r}(x,y)b^{n}(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-s}\psi^{j}_{s}(y))\,dydr\|_{L_{m}}\leqslant C\|b^{n}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{0}_{1}}|t-s|^{\frac{3}{4}}

for a universal constant
C>0
. By the Lebesgue monotone convergence theorem, we have
\lim_{j\to\infty}P_{r-s}\psi^{j}_{s}(y)=P_{r-s}\psi_{s}(y)
for every
r,s,y
. Then by the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem, we see that the left-hand side above converges to
\|A^{T,n}_{s,t}(x)\|_{L_{m}}
as
j\to\infty
. Since
\|b^{n}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{0}_{1}}\leqslant\|b\|_{\mathcal{B}^{0}_{1}}
, this implies (
5.38
).

Step 3.
We show by mean of
4.7
that for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
(s,t)\in\Delta_{0,T_{0}}
there exist a non-negative measurable map
(x,\omega)\mapsto L_{s,t}^{n}(x,\omega)
and a deterministic finite constant
C
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},x\in{D}}\|L^{n}_{s,t}(x)\|_{L_{m}}\leqslant C(t-s)^{\frac{3}{4}}

and

K^{n}_{t}(x)-P_{t-s}K^{n}_{s}(x)\leqslant C[K_{t}(x)-P_{t-s}K_{s}(x)](t-s)^{\frac{3}{4}}+L^{n}_{s,t}(x)

(5.39)

for every
(s,t)\in\Delta_{0,T_{0}}

x\in{D}
.

Fix
x\in{D}
,
T\in[0,T_{0}]
. We define

\lambda^{T}_{s,t}(x)=P_{T-t}K_{t}(x)-P_{T-s}K_{s}(x),\quad(s,t)\in\Delta_{0,T}.

From (
5.37
), we see that
0\leqslant\lambda^{T}_{s,t}(x)\leqslant K_{T}(x)<\infty
. It is evident that
\lambda
is additive, that is
\lambda^{T}_{s,t}(x)=\lambda^{T}_{s,u}(x)+\lambda^{T}_{u,t}(x)
for every
s\leqslant u\leqslant t
. Hence,
(s,t)\mapsto\lambda^{T}_{s,t}(x)
is a random control per
4.6
.

Define
A^{T,n}_{s,t}(x)
as in the previous step
and

\mathcal{A}^{T,n}_{t}(x):=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{D}p_{T-r}(x,y)b^{n}(V_{r}(y)+\psi_{r}(y))\,dydr=P_{T-t}K^{n}_{t}(x).

Then for
u:=(s+t)/2
we have

\delta A^{T,n}_{s,u,t}(x)=\int_{u}^{t}\int_{D}p_{T-r}(x,y)[b^{n}(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-s}\psi_{s}(y))-b^{n}(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-u}\psi_{u}(y))]\,dydr.

Applying consequently the Fubini theorem, (
C.7
),
Lemma

A.3
(iv) and
Lemma

C.3
, we deduce that

\displaystyle|\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{u}\delta A^{T,n}_{s,u,t}(x)|

\displaystyle=\Bigl|\int_{u}^{t}\int_{D}p_{T-r}(x,y)\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{u}[b^{n}(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-s}\psi_{s}(y))-b^{n}(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-u}\psi_{u}(y))]\,dydr\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{u}^{t}\int_{D}p_{T-r}(x,y)\|G_{\rho_{r-u}(x)}b^{n}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}|P_{r-s}\psi_{s}(y)-P_{r-u}\psi_{u}(y)|\,dydr

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|b^{n}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{0}_{1}}\int_{u}^{t}\int_{D}p_{T-r}(x,y)|P_{r-u}\psi_{u}(y)-P_{r-s}\psi_{s}(y)|(r-u)^{-1/2}\,dydr,

where we used the notation
\rho_{t}(x):=\mathop{\mbox{\rm Var}}(V_{t}(x))
. Since
\psi=Pu_{0}+K
, we see that
P_{r-u}\psi_{u}-P_{r-s}\psi_{s}=P_{r-u}K_{u}-P_{r-s}K_{s}
.
Using the elementary inequality

|P_{r-u}K_{u}(y)-P_{r-s}K_{s}(y)|\leqslant P_{r-u}|K_{u}(\cdot)-P_{u-s}K_{s}(\cdot)|(y)

and (
5.37
), we get

\displaystyle|\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{u}\delta A^{T,n}_{s,u,t}(x)|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|b^{n}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{0}_{1}}\int_{u}^{t}\int_{D}p_{T-u}(x,y)[K_{u}(y)-P_{u-s}K_{s}(y)](r-u)^{-1/2}\,dydr

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|b^{n}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{0}_{1}}\lambda_{s,u}^{T}(x)(t-u)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

As we have shown previously,
(s,t)\mapsto\lambda^{T}_{s,t}(x)
is a random control for every fixed
T,x
. Hence, the above estimate verifies condition (
4.13
). The estimate (
5.38
) verifies condition (
4.3
) (with
\alpha_{2}=\beta_{2}=0
and
n=m
).
It remains to verify condition (
4.4
). Let
\Pi:=\{0=t_{0},t_{1},...,t_{k}=T\}
be an arbitrary partition of
[0,T]
. Denote by
|\Pi|
its mesh size. Then we have

\displaystyle\Bigl|\mathcal{A}_{t}-\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}A_{t_{i},t_{i+1}}\Bigr|

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\!\!\!\int_{D}p_{T-r}(x,y)\bigl|b^{n}(V_{r}(y)+\psi_{r}(y))-b^{n}(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-{t_{i}}}\psi_{t_{i}}(y))\bigr|\,dydr.

For each
i
, using the fact that
b^{n}
is Lipschitz, we have

\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\!\!\!\int_{D}p_{T-r}(x,y)\bigl|b^{n}(V_{r}(y)+\psi_{r}(y))-b^{n}(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-{t_{i}}}\psi_{t_{i}}(y))\bigr|\,dydr\\
\leqslant\|b^{n}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\!\!\!\int_{D}p_{T-r}(x,y)|\psi_{r}(y)-P_{r-{t_{i}}}\psi_{t_{i}}(y)|\,dydr.

Since
\psi=Pu_{0}+K
, we have
|\psi_{r}(y)-P_{r-{t_{i}}}\psi_{t_{i}}(y)|=|K_{r}(y)-P_{r-{t_{i}}}K_{t_{i}}(y)|=K_{r}(y)-P_{r-{t_{i}}}K_{t_{i}}(y)
where we have used (
5.37
). Hence, combining with the above estimate and applying (
5.37
) once again yield

\displaystyle\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\!\!\!\int_{D}p_{T-r}(x,y)

\displaystyle\bigl|b^{n}(V_{r}(y)+\psi_{r}(y))-b^{n}(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-{t_{i}}}\psi_{t_{i}}(y))\bigr|\,dydr

\displaystyle\leqslant\|b^{n}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}[P_{T-r}K_{r}(x)-P_{T-{t_{i}}}K_{t_{i}}(x)]dr

\displaystyle\leqslant\|b^{n}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}[P_{T-t_{i+1}}K_{t_{i+1}}(x)-P_{T-{t_{i}}}K_{t_{i}}(x)](t_{i+1}-t_{i}).

It follows that

\displaystyle\Bigl|\mathcal{A}_{t}-\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}A_{t_{i},t_{i+1}}\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|b^{n}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}\sum_{i}[P_{T-t_{i+1}}K_{t_{i+1}}(x)-P_{T-{t_{i}}}K_{t_{i}}(x)](t_{i+1}-t_{i})

\displaystyle\leqslant\|b^{n}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}(K_{T}(x)-P_{T}K_{0}(x))|\Pi|

which converges to
0
a.s. as
|\Pi|\to 0
. Thus, condition (
4.4
) holds.

Applying
4.7
, we have

\displaystyle|P_{T-t}K^{n}_{t}(x)-P_{T-s}K^{n}_{s}(x)|

\displaystyle\leqslant C[P_{T-t}K_{t}(x)-P_{T-s}K_{s}(x)](t-s)^{\frac{1}{2}}+B^{T,n}_{s,t}(x)+|A^{T,n}_{s,t}(x)|

where
\|B^{T,n}_{s,t}(x)\|_{{L_{m}}}\leqslant C|t-s|^{3/4}
uniformly in
T,n,x
.
To obtain (
5.39
), it suffices to put
T=t
,
L^{n}_{s,t}(x)=B^{t,n}_{s,t}(x)+|A^{t,n}_{s,t}(x)|
.
Since
B^{T,n}
is a functional of
\delta A^{T,n}(x)
, measurability of
(x,\omega)\mapsto L^{n}_{s,t}
follows. The uniform moment estimate for
L^{n}_{s,t}(x)
follows from those of
B^{T,n}_{s,t}(x)
and
A^{T,n}_{s,t}(x)
in (
5.38
).

Step 4.
We show that
\lim_{n\to\infty}P_{r}K^{n}_{t}(x)=P_{r}K_{t}(x)
in probability for every
t\in[0,T_{0}]
,
r\in[0,T_{0}-t]
and
x\in{D}
.

The situation here is similar to
Lemma

B.5
except for a uniform moment bound for
K^{n}_{t}(x)
. We replace this condition by estimate (
5.39
) and the uniform moment bound for
L_{0,t}^{n}(x)
.
Indeed, let
x\in{D}
be fixed and let
M>0
be a positive number. We write

P_{r}K^{n}_{t}(x)=\int_{|y|\leqslant M}p_{r}(x,y)K^{n}_{t}(y)dy+\int_{|y|>M}p_{r}(x,y)K^{n}_{t}(y)dy.

Since
K^{n}_{t}
converges to
K_{t}
uniformly on
\{y\in{D}:|y|\leqslant M\}
, we see that
\int_{|y|\leqslant M}p_{r}(x,y)K^{n}_{t}(y)dy
converges to
\int_{|y|\leqslant M}p_{r}(x,y)K_{t}(y)dy
a.s. To treat the second term, we first set
s=0
in (
5.39
) to obtain that

|K^{n}_{t}(y)|\leqslant Ct^{\frac{1}{2}}K_{t}(y)+L^{n}_{0,t}(y)\quad\textrm{a.s.}\quad\forall y\in{D}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\int_{|y|>M}p_{r}(x,y)K^{n}_{t}(y)dy\leqslant Ct^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{|y|>M}p_{r}(x,y)K_{t}(y)dy+\int_{|y|>M}p_{r}(x,y)L^{n}_{0,t}(y)dy.

By the Lebesgue monotone convergence theorem and (
5.37
), we see that a.s.

\lim_{M\to\infty}\int_{|y|\leqslant M}p_{r}(x,y)K_{t}(y)dy=\int_{D}p_{r}(x,y)K_{t}(y)dy\leqslant K_{t+r}(x)<\infty

so that a.s.

\lim_{M\to\infty}\int_{|y|>M}p_{r}(x,y)K_{t}(y)dy=0.

Lastly,

\displaystyle\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt\int_{|y|>M}p_{r}(x,y)L^{n}_{0,t}(y)dy\leqslant\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},z\in{D}}\|L^{n}_{0,t}(z)\|_{L_{m}}\left(\int_{|y|>M}p_{r}(x,y)dy\right)

which converges to
0
as
M\to\infty
. These facts imply the claim.

Step 5.

We define
L_{s,t}(x)=\liminf_{n}L^{n}_{s,t}(x)
then by Fatou’s lemma and Step
3,
\|L_{s,t}(x)\|_{{L_{m}}}\leqslant C|t-s|^{3/4}
uniformly in
x
.
In (
5.39
), we send
n\to\infty
, applying the convergence in step 4 to obtain that

\displaystyle K_{t}(x)-P_{t-s}K_{s}(x)\leqslant C(K_{t}(x)-P_{t-s}K_{s}(x))(t-s)^{\frac{1}{2}}+L_{s,t}(x).

This implies that
K_{t}(x)-P_{t-s}K_{s}(x)\leqslant 2L_{s,t}(x)
for
t-s\leqslant\ell
and
\ell
is such that
C\ell^{1/2}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}
. An application of
L_{m}
-norm yields

\displaystyle\|K_{t}(x)-P_{t-s}K_{s}(x)\|_{L_{m}}\leqslant 2\|L_{s,t}(x)\|_{L_{m}}\leqslant 2C|t-s|^{3/4}

for every
t-s\leqslant\ell
. Using the identity
K_{t}-P_{t-s}K_{s}=\psi_{t}-P_{t-s}\psi_{s}
once again and recalling that
\psi=u-V
, the above estimate shows that
u-V
belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{3/4,0}L_{m}([S,T])
. This completes the proof of
Proposition

3.8
.

6
Proofs of regularity lemmas

In this section, we present the proofs of Lemmas
5.1
,
5.2
,
5.5
,
5.6
and
5.7
.
Throughout the section, we fix the filtration
(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\geqslant 0}
, which appears in the aforementioned lemmas.

6.1
Proof of Lemmas
5.1
and
5.2

We begin with the following auxiliary result, which will be crucial also for the proof of
Proposition
5.7
. The proof relies on the stochastic sewing lemma (
4.1
) and the estimates in
Lemmas

C.3
and
C.4
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
0\leqslant S\leqslant T
. Let
\gamma\in(-2,0)
,
m\in[2,\infty)
,
n\in[m,\infty]
and
p\in[n,\infty]
.
Let
h\colon\mathbb{R}\times[S,T]\times{D}\times\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
be a globally bounded measurable function. Let
X:[S,T]\times{D}\to\mathbb{R}
be a measurable function. Suppose further that the following conditions hold:

1.

for any fixed
(z,r,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times[S,T]\times{D}

the random variable
h(z,r,x)
is
\mathcal{F}_{S}
-measurable;

2.

there exists a constant
\Gamma_{h}>0
such that

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in[S,T]\\
x\in{D}\end{subarray}}\|\,\|h(\cdot,r,x)\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\|_{L_{n}}\leqslant\Gamma_{h};

(6.1)

3.

there exist constants
\Gamma_{X}>0
,
\nu\in[0,\frac{1}{2})
such that
for every
r\in[S,T]
,
x\in{D}

\int_{D}|X_{r}(y)|dy\leqslant\Gamma_{X}(T-r)^{-\nu}.

(6.2)

Then there exists a constant
C=C(\gamma,m,n,p)
which does not depend on
S
,
T
,
\Gamma_{h}
,
\Gamma_{X}
,
h
,
K
such that for any
t\in[S,T]

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\Bigl\|\int_{S}^{t}\int_{D}X_{r}(y)h(V_{r}(y),r,y)\,dydr\Bigr\|_{{L_{m}}|\mathcal{F}_{S}}\Bigr\|_{{L_{n}}}\leqslant C\Gamma_{h}\Gamma_{X}(T-S)^{-\nu}(t-S)^{1+\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}}.

(6.3)

Proof.

The proof is based on the stochastic sewing lemma, Theorem
4.1
.
We put for
S\leqslant s<t\leqslant T
,

A_{s,t}:=\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{s}\int_{s}^{t}\int_{D}X_{r}(y)h(V_{r}(y),r,y)\,dydr.

Put also for
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times{D}

\mathcal{A}_{t}=\int_{S}^{t}\int_{D}X_{r}(y)h(V_{r}(y),r,y,\omega)\,dydr.

Note that the integral in the left-hand side of inequality (
6.3
) is just

\mathcal{A}_{t}-\mathcal{A}_{S}
.

Let us verify that all the conditions of
4.1
are satisfied. For any
S\leqslant s<t\leqslant T
,
u:=(s+t)/2
we have

\displaystyle\delta A_{s,u,t}=\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{s}\int_{u}^{t}\int_{D}X_{r}(y)h(V_{r}(y),r,y)\,dydr-\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{u}\int_{u}^{t}\int_{D}X_{r}(y)h(V_{r}(y),r,y)\,dydr.

Therefore

\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{s}\delta A_{s,u,t}=0

and thus condition (
4.2
) holds.

Further, it is easy to see by the conditional Jensen’s inequality and Minkowski’s integral inequality that for any
x\in{D}
,
S\leqslant s<t\leqslant T
and
u:=(s+t)/2

\displaystyle\|\delta A_{s,u,t}\|_{L_{n}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\Bigl\|\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{u}\int_{u}^{t}\int_{D}X_{r}(y)h(V_{r}(y),r,y)\,dydr\Bigr\|_{L_{n}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\int_{u}^{t}\int_{D}|X_{r}(y)|\|\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{u}h(V_{r}(y),r,y)\|_{L_{n}}\,dydr.

(6.4)

Now for fixed
r\in[u,t]
,
y\in{D}
we apply bound (
C.8
) to the function
f\colon(z,\omega)\mapsto h(z,r,y,\omega)
which is
\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R})\otimes\mathcal{F}_{S}
-measurable. We get

\displaystyle\|\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{u}h(V_{r}(y),r,y)\|_{L_{n}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|\,\|h(\cdot,r,y)\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\|_{L_{n}}(r-u)^{\frac{\gamma}{4}}(u-S)^{-\frac{1}{2p}}(r-S)^{\frac{1}{4p}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|\,\|h(\cdot,r,y)\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\|_{L_{n}}(r-u)^{\frac{\gamma}{4}}(u-S)^{-\frac{1}{4p}},

(6.5)

where the last inequality follows because
r-S\leqslant 2(u-S)
.
Substituting (
6.5
) into (
6.1
) and using (
6.1
), (
6.2
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\|\delta A_{s,u,t}\|_{L_{n}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\Gamma_{h}\Gamma_{X}(u-S)^{-\frac{1}{4p}}(T-u)^{-\nu}(t-u)^{1+\frac{\gamma}{4}},

where the last inequality follows from (
B.7
). From the assumptions
\nu\in(0,1/2)
,
\gamma>-2
,
p\geqslant 2
, we see that condition (
4.7
) holds
with
\alpha_{2}=1/(4p)<1/2
,
\beta_{2}=\nu<1/2
,
\varepsilon_{2}=1/2+\gamma/4>0
. Hence, by
4.3
inequality (
4.3
) is satisfied.

Finally, let us check condition (
4.4
). Let
\Pi:=\{S=t_{0},t_{1},...,t_{k}=t\}
be an arbitrary partition of
[S,t]
. Denote by
|\Pi|
its mesh size. Note that for any
i\in[0,k-1]
we have
\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt_{t_{i}}(\mathcal{A}_{t_{i+1}}-\mathcal{A}_{t_{i}}-A_{t_{i},t_{i+1}})=0
and
\mathcal{A}_{t_{i+1}}-\mathcal{A}_{t_{i}}-A_{t_{i},t_{i+1}}
is
\mathcal{F}_{t_{i+1}}
–measurable. Therefore,
\mathcal{A}_{t}-\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}A_{t_{i},t_{i+1}}
is a sum of martingale differences. Then using orthogonality,

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\mathcal{A}_{t}-\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}A_{t_{i},t_{i+1}}\Bigr\|_{{L_{2}}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\|\mathcal{A}_{t_{i+1}}-\mathcal{A}_{t_{i}}-A_{t_{i},t_{i+1}}\|_{{L_{2}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|h\|_{L_{\infty}}\Gamma_{X}\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\Bigl(\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}(T-r)^{-\nu}\,dr\Bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|h\|_{L_{\infty}}\Gamma_{X}(T-S)^{1-\nu}\max_{i=0..(k-1)}\bigl|\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}(T-r)^{-\nu}\,dr\bigr|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|h\|_{L_{\infty}}\Gamma_{X}(T-S)^{1-\nu}|\Pi|^{1-\nu}.

Therefore,
\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}A_{t_{i},t_{i+1}}
converges to
\mathcal{A}_{t}
in probability as
|\Pi|\to\infty
and thus condition (
4.4
) holds.

Thus, all the conditions of
4.1
are satisfied. Hence, by inequality
(
4.5
), taking into account (
B.7
) and (
3.2
) we have for any
t\in[S,T]
,
x\in{D}

\|\|\mathcal{A}_{t}-\mathcal{A}_{S}\|_{{L_{m}}|\mathcal{F}_{S}}\|_{L_{n}}\leqslant\|A_{S,t}\|_{L_{n}}+C\Gamma_{h}\Gamma_{X}(T-S)^{-\nu}(t-S)^{1+\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}}.

(6.6)

Applying (
C.9
) to the function
f\colon(z,\omega)\mapsto h(z,r,y,\omega)
, we get

\displaystyle\|A_{S,t}\|_{L_{n}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{S}^{t}\int_{D}|X_{r}(y)|\,\|\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{S}h(V_{r}(y),r,y)\|_{L_{n}}\,dydr

\displaystyle\leqslant C\int_{S}^{t}\int_{D}|X_{r}(y)|\,\|\,\|h(\cdot,r,y)\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\|_{L_{n}}(r-S)^{\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}}\,dydr

\displaystyle\leqslant C\Gamma_{h}\Gamma_{X}(T-S)^{-\nu}(t-S)^{1+\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}},

where
in the last inequality we made use of (
6.1
), (
6.2
) and (
B.7
). Combining this with (
6.6
), we finally obtain (
6.3
).
∎

An important consequence of
6.1
is the following statement.

Corollary 6.2
.

Let
f\in\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}
be a bounded function,
\gamma\in(-2,0)
,
m\in[2,\infty)
,
p\in[m,\infty]
.

1.

Let
X:[0,T]\times{D}\to\mathbb{R}
be a measurable function satisfying (
6.2
) for some
\Gamma_{X}>0
,
\nu\in[0,\frac{1}{2})
. Then there exists a constant
C=C(\gamma,m,p,\nu)
such that for any
0\leqslant s\leqslant t\leqslant T
and any
\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R})\otimes\mathcal{F}_{s}
-measurable function
\kappa\in{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}
one has

\Bigl\|\Bigl\|\int_{s}^{t}\int_{D}X_{r}(y)f(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-s}\kappa(y))\,dydr\Bigr\|_{{L_{m}}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}\Bigr\|_{{L_{p}}}\\
\leqslant C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\Gamma_{X}(T-s)^{-\nu}(t-s)^{1+\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}}.

(6.7)

2.

Let
\lambda\in(0,1]
and assume that

\gamma>-2+\lambda.

(6.8)

Then there exists a constant

C=C(\gamma,\lambda,m,p)
such that for any
0\leqslant s\leqslant t\leqslant T_{1}
and any
\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R})\otimes\mathcal{F}_{s}
-measurable functions
\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}
one has

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in{D}}\Bigl\|\int_{s}^{t}\int_{D}p_{T-r}(x,y)\bigl[f(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-s}\kappa_{1}(y))-f(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-s}\kappa_{2}(y))\bigr]dr\Bigr\|_{{L_{m}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leqslant C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\|\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}\|^{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}(t-s)^{1+\frac{\gamma-\lambda}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}}.

(6.9)

3.

Let
\lambda,\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in(0,1]
. Assume that (
6.8
) holds and that

\gamma>-2+\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}.

(6.10)

Then there exists a constant

C=C(\gamma,\lambda,\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},p)
such that for any
0\leqslant s\leqslant u\leqslant t\leqslant 1
, any
\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R})\otimes\mathcal{F}_{s}
measurable functions
\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}
, any
\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R})\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}
measurable functions
\kappa_{3},\kappa_{4}\in{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}
one has

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in{D}}\Bigl\|\int_{u}^{t}\int_{D}p_{T-r}(x,y)\bigl[f(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-u}\kappa_{1}(y))-f(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-u}\kappa_{2}(y))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-f(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-u}\kappa_{3}(y))+f(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-u}\kappa_{4}(y))\bigr]dr\Bigr\|_{{L_{m}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leqslant C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\sup_{y\in{D}}\|\|\kappa_{1}(y)-\kappa_{3}(y)\|_{{L_{m}}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}\|_{{L_{\infty}}}^{\lambda_{2}}\,\|\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}\|^{\lambda_{1}}_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}(t-u)^{1+\frac{\gamma-\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}}

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{\leqslant}+C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\|\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-\kappa_{3}+\kappa_{4}\|^{\lambda}_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}(t-u)^{1+\frac{\gamma-\lambda}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}}.

(6.11)

Proof.

The results are obtained by choosing suitable
n
,
K
and
h
in
6.1
. In what follows, we fix
0\leqslant s\leqslant u\leqslant t\leqslant T
and
x
in
{D}
. We note that the expressions
P_{r}\kappa_{i}(y)
,
i=1,..,4
are well defined thanks to
B.4
and the assumption
\kappa_{i}\in{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}
.

(i) We take
n=p
,
h(z,r,y,\omega):=f(z+P_{r-s}[\kappa(\cdot,\omega)](y))
, where
z\in\mathbb{R}
,
r\in[s,t]
,
y\in{D}
,
\omega\in\Omega
. Let us verify that
h
satisfies all the assumptions of
6.1
with
s
in place of
S
. Since
\kappa
is
\mathcal{F}_{s}
-measurable, we see that the random variable
h(z,r,x)
is
\mathcal{F}_{s}
measurable and the first assumption of
6.1
holds.
It follows from (
A.4
) that

\|f(\cdot+P_{r-s}\kappa(y))\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\leqslant\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}.

Hence (
6.1
) holds with
\Gamma_{h}:=\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}
.
Thus all the conditions of
6.1
are met. Now (
6.7
) follows directly from inequality (
6.3
).

(ii) We fix
x\in{D}
, choose
n=m
and
X_{r}(y):=p_{T-r}(x,y)
. Let us apply
6.1
for the function

h\colon(z,r,y,\omega)\mapsto f(z+P_{r-s}\kappa_{1}(y))-f(z+P_{r-s}\kappa_{2}(y)),\quad z\in\mathbb{R},r\in[s,t],y\in{D},\omega\in\Omega.

It is easy to see that (
6.2
) is satisfied with
\Gamma_{X}=1
and
\nu=0
. Let us verify that
h
satisfies all the assumptions of
6.1
with
s
in place of
S
. The first assumption clearly holds. To check the second assumption, we note that (
A.5
) implies

\|f(\cdot+P_{r-s}\kappa_{1}(y))-f(\cdot+P_{r-s}\kappa_{2}(y))\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma-\lambda}_{p}}\leqslant\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}|P_{r-s}\kappa_{1}(y)-P_{r-s}\kappa_{2}(y)|^{\lambda}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\,\|h(\cdot,r,y)\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma-\lambda}_{p}}\|_{{L_{m}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\|P_{r-s}\kappa_{1}(y)-P_{r-s}\kappa_{2}(y)\|_{{L_{m}}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\sup_{z\in{D}}\|\kappa_{1}(z)-\kappa_{2}(z)\|_{{L_{m}}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\|\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}^{\lambda},

where in the first inequality we used the fact that
\||\xi|^{\lambda}\|_{{L_{m}}}\leqslant\|\xi\|_{{L_{m}}}^{\lambda}
for any random variable
\xi
and
\lambda\in(0,1]
; the second inequality follows from (
B.8
); the third inequality follows from the definition of the norm
\|\cdot\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}
, see (
3.5
).
Thus, inequality (
6.1
) is satisfied with
\Gamma_{h}:=\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\|\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}^{\lambda}
.
Thus all the conditions of
6.1
are met. Bound (
2
) follows now from (
6.3
) and (
3.2
).

(iii). We fix
x\in{D}
, choose
n=m
,
X_{r}(y):=p_{T-r}(x,y)
and

h(z,r,y,\omega):=f(z+P_{r-u}\kappa_{1}(y))-f(z+P_{r-u}\kappa_{2}(y))\\
\quad-f(z+P_{r-u}\kappa_{3}(y))+f(z+[P_{r-u}(\kappa_{3}+\kappa_{2}-\kappa_{1})](y)),

where
z\in\mathbb{R}
,
r\in[u,t]
,
y\in{D}
,
\omega\in\Omega
. Let us verify
h
satisfies the assumptions of
6.1
with
u
in place of
S
.

Again, it is easy to see that the first and the third conditions of
6.1
are satisfied with
\Gamma_{X}=1
and
\nu=0
. To check the second condition, we note that (
A.6
) yields

\|h(\cdot,r,y)\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma-\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}}_{p}}\leqslant\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}|P_{r-u}\kappa_{1}(y)-P_{r-u}\kappa_{2}(y)|^{\lambda_{1}}\,|P_{r-u}\kappa_{1}(y)-P_{r-u}\kappa_{3}(y)|^{\lambda_{2}}.

Using the fact that the functions
\kappa_{1}
and
\kappa_{2}
are
\mathcal{F}_{s}
-measurable, we derive from the above bound

\displaystyle\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt\|h(\cdot,r,y)\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma-\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}}_{p}}^{m}=\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{s}\|h(\cdot,r,y)\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma-\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}}_{p}}^{m}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}^{m}\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt\Bigl[|P_{r-u}\kappa_{1}(y)-P_{r-u}\kappa_{2}(y)|^{m\lambda_{1}}\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{s}|P_{r-u}(\kappa_{1}(y))-P_{r-u}\kappa_{3}(y)|^{m\lambda_{2}}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}^{m}\|\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{s}|P_{r-u}\kappa_{1}(y)-P_{r-u}\kappa_{3}(y)|^{m\lambda_{2}}\|_{{L_{\infty}}}\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt\bigl[|P_{r-u}\kappa_{1}(y)-P_{r-u}\kappa_{2}(y)|^{m\lambda_{1}}\bigr]

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}^{m}\|\,\|P_{r-u}[\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{3}](y)\|_{{L_{m}}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}\|_{{L_{\infty}}}^{m\lambda_{2}}\,\,\|P_{r-u}[\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}](y)\|_{L_{m}}^{m\lambda_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}^{m}\sup_{y\in{D}}\|\|\kappa_{1}(y)-\kappa_{3}(y)\|_{{L_{m}}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}\|_{{L_{\infty}}}^{m\lambda_{2}}\,\|\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}\|^{m\lambda_{1}}_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}.

Here in the penultimate inequality we used the fact that

\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{s}[|\xi|^{\lambda}]\leqslant(\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{s}|\xi|)^{\lambda}
for any random variable
\xi
and
\lambda\in(0,1]
,
s\geqslant 0
; in the last inequality we applied bound (
B.8
).

Thus, inequality (
6.1
) is satisfied with

\Gamma_{h}:=\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\sup_{y\in{D}}\|\|\kappa_{1}(y)-\kappa_{3}(y)\|_{{L_{m}}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}\|_{{L_{\infty}}}^{\lambda_{2}}\,\|\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}\|^{\lambda_{1}}_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}.

Bound (
3
) follows now from
6.1
and
part
2
of the Corollary.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

5.1
.

In
6.2
1
, we choose
X_{r}(y)=P_{T-r}(x,y)
. In this case, condition (
6.2
) is satisfied with
\Gamma_{X}=1
and
\nu=0
. Hence the estimates (
6.7
) and (
3.2
) imply (
5.1
).
∎

To establish
5.2
, we need the following result.

Lemma 6.3
.

Assume that all the conditions of
5.2
are satisfied.
Let
X:[0,T]\times{D}\to\mathbb{R}
be a measurable function satisfying (
6.2
) for some
\Gamma_{X}>0
,
\nu\in[0,\frac{1}{2})
.
Then there exists a constant
C=C(\gamma,p,\tau,m,n)>0
such that for any
S\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\,\Bigl\|\int_{S}^{T}\int_{D}X_{r}(y)f(V_{r}(y)+\psi_{r}(y))\,dydr\Bigr\|_{{L_{m}}|\mathcal{F}_{S}}\Bigr\|_{{L_{n}}}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\Gamma_{X}(T-S)^{1+\frac{1}{4}(\gamma-\frac{1}{p})-\nu}+C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\Gamma_{X}[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{\tau,0}L_{m,n}({[S,T]})}}(T-S)^{\frac{3}{4}+\frac{1}{4}(\gamma-\frac{1}{p})+\tau-\nu}.

(6.12)

Proof.

The proof of the lemma is based on the Stochastic Sewing lemma (
4.1
),
6.2
1
, and dominated convergence arguments.

We can assume without loss of generality that
[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{\tau,0}L_{m,n}({[S,T]})}}<\infty
. Indeed, otherwise the right-hand side of (
6.3
) is infinite. Therefore, recalling (
3.2
), we have
[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{\tau,0}L_{m}({[S,T]})}}<\infty
. Thus for any
s\in[S,T]

we have

\psi_{s}\in{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}.

(6.13)

Step 1
. Assume first that

f\in\mathcal{C}^{1}_{b}
. Fix
0\leqslant S\leqslant T
and put for
(s,t)\in\Delta_{S,T}

A_{s,t}:=\int_{s}^{t}\int_{D}X_{r}(y)f(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-s}\psi_{s}(y))\,dydr.

Note that the expression
P_{r-s}\psi_{s}(y)
is well-defined thanks to
B.4
and (
6.13
). Set now

\mathcal{A}_{t}=\int_{S}^{t}\int_{D}X_{r}(y)f(V_{r}(y)+\psi_{r}(y))dydr.

It is easy to see that the integral in the left-hand side of inequality (
6.3
) is
\mathcal{A}_{T}-\mathcal{A}_{S}
.
Let us verify that all the conditions of
4.1
are satisfied.

Clearly, for any
S\leqslant s<u<t\leqslant T
we get

\delta A_{s,u,t}=\int_{u}^{t}\int_{D}X_{r}(y)[f(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-s}\psi_{s}(y))-f(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-u}\psi_{u}(y))]\,dydr.

For fixed
S\leqslant s<u<r\leqslant T
,
y\in{D}
introduce a function
h_{r,y}\colon\mathbb{R}\times\Omega\to\mathbb{R}

h_{r,y}\colon(z,\omega)\mapsto f(z+P_{r-s}\psi_{s}(y))-f(z+P_{r-u}\psi_{u}(y)).

Note that for fixed non-random parameters the random variable
h_{r,y}(z)
is
\mathcal{F}_{u}
-measurable. Hence, applying (
C.9
) with
u,r
in place of
s,t
, respectively, we deduce

\displaystyle|\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{s}\delta A_{s,u,t}|

\displaystyle=\Bigl|\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{s}\int_{u}^{t}\int_{D}X_{r}(y)\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{u}h_{r,y}(V_{r}(y))\,dydr\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\int_{u}^{t}\int_{D}|X_{r}(y)|(r-u)^{-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}}\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{s}\|h_{r,y}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma-1}_{p}}\,dydr

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\int_{u}^{t}\int_{D}|X_{r}(y)|(r-u)^{-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}}\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{s}|P_{r-s}\psi_{s}(y)-P_{r-u}\psi_{u}(y)|\,dydr,

where the last inequality follows from (
A.5
) with
\lambda=1
.
Applying the integral Minkowski inequality, we derive

\!\|\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{s}\delta A_{s,u,t}\|_{{L_{n}}}\!\leqslant C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\!\int_{u}^{t}\!\!\int_{D}\!\!\!|X_{r}(y)|(r-u)^{-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}}\bigl\|\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{s}|P_{r-s}\psi_{s}(y)-P_{r-u}\psi_{u}(y)|\bigr\|_{{L_{n}}}\,dydr.

(6.14)

Using the fact that for any
s\geqslant 0
and random variable
\xi
one has
\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{s}|\xi|\leqslant\|\xi\|_{{L_{m}}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}
we deduce for any
y\in{D}
,

\displaystyle\|\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{s}|P_{r-s}\psi_{s}(y)-P_{r-u}\psi_{u}(y)|\|_{{L_{n}}}

\displaystyle=\|\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{s}|[P_{r-u}(P_{u-s}\psi_{s}-\psi_{u})](y)|\|_{{L_{n}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\|[P_{r-u}(P_{u-s}\psi_{s}-\psi_{u})](y)\|_{{L_{m}}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}\|_{{L_{n}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sup_{z\in{D}}\|\|P_{u-s}\psi_{s}(z)-\psi_{u}(z)|\mathcal{F}_{s}\|_{{L_{m}}}\|_{{L_{n}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{\tau,0}L_{m,n}({[S,T]})}}(u-s)^{\tau},

where the penultimate inequality follows from (
B.8
), and in the last inequality we used the definition of the seminorm
[\cdot]_{{\mathcal{C}^{\tau,0}L_{m,n}({[S,T]})}}
given in (
3.3
). Substituting this into (
6.14
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{s}\delta A_{s,u,t}\|_{{L_{n}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{\tau,0}L_{m,n}({[S,T]})}}(u-s)^{\tau}\int_{u}^{t}\int_{D}|X_{r}(y)|(r-u)^{-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}}\,dydr

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\Gamma_{X}[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{\tau,0}L_{m,n}({[S,T]})}}(T-u)^{-\nu}(t-s)^{\frac{3}{4}+\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}+\tau},

where the last inequality follows from assumption (
6.2
) and
inequality (
B.7
). Since, by assumption,
\gamma/4-1/(4p)+\tau>1/4
, we see that condition (
4.2
) of
4.1
holds with
\alpha_{1}=0
,
\beta_{1}=\nu<1/2
,
\varepsilon_{1}=\gamma/4-1/(4p)+\tau-1/4>0
.

Now we move on to the verification of condition (
4.3
). Applying
6.2
1
to the function
k:=\psi_{s}
, we deduce that for any
(s,t)\in\Delta_{S,T}

\|\|A_{s,t}\|_{{L_{m}}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}\|_{{L_{n}}}\leqslant\|\|A_{s,t}\|_{{L_{m}}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}\|_{{L_{p}}}\leqslant C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\Gamma_{X}(T-s)^{-\nu}(t-s)^{1+\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}}.

(6.15)

This induces that for any
(s,t)\in\Delta_{S,T}
and
u\in[s,t]
one has

\|\|\delta A_{s,u,t}\|_{{L_{m}}|\mathcal{F}_{s}}\|_{{L_{n}}}\leqslant C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{p}}\Gamma_{X}(T-u)^{-\nu}(t-s)^{1+\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}}.

Since
\gamma-\frac{1}{p}>-2
by assumption, we see that condition (
4.3
) holds with
\alpha_{2}=0
,
\beta_{2}=\nu<1/2
,
\varepsilon_{2}=1/2+\gamma/4-1/(4p)>0
.

Finally, let us check condition (
4.4
). Let
\Pi:=\{S=t_{0},t_{1},...,t_{k}=t\}
be an arbitrary partition of
[S,t]
. Denote by
|\Pi|
its mesh size. Note that contrary to the proof of
6.1
, we cannot use here the orthogonality because the sum
\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}(\mathcal{A}_{t_{i+1}}-\mathcal{A}_{t_{i}}-A_{t_{i},t_{i+1}})
is not a sum of martingale differences. Indeed, in contrast to the proof of
6.1
,
\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt_{t_{i}}(\mathcal{A}_{t_{i+1}}-\mathcal{A}_{t_{i}})\neq A_{t_{i},t_{i+1}}
. Nevertheless, we have

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\mathcal{A}_{t}-\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}A_{t_{i},t_{i+1}}\Bigr\|_{{L_{m}}}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\!\!\!\int_{D}|X_{r}(y)|\bigl\|f(V_{r}(y)+\psi_{r}(y))-f(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-{t_{i}}}\psi_{t_{i}}(y))\bigr\|_{{L_{m}}}\,dydr

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant C\Gamma_{X}(1+T)\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{\tau,0}L_{m}({[S,T]})}}|\Pi|^{\tau}.

This verifies condition (
4.4
).

We see that for
f\in\mathcal{C}_{b}^{1}
all the conditions of
4.1
are satisfied. Thus, by
(
4.5
), taking into account (
4.1
), we have

\displaystyle\|\|\mathcal{A}_{T}-\mathcal{A}_{S}\|_{{L_{m}}|\mathcal{F}_{S}}\|_{{L_{n}}}\leqslant

\displaystyle\|\|A_{S,T}\|_{{L_{m}}|\mathcal{F}_{S}}\|_{{L_{n}}}+C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\Gamma_{X}(T-S)^{1+\frac{1}{4}(\gamma-\frac{1}{p})-\nu}

\displaystyle+C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\Gamma_{X}[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{\tau,0}L_{m,n}({[S,T]})}}(T-S)^{\frac{3}{4}+\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{4p}+\tau-\nu}\,,

which together with (
6.15
) implies (
6.3
).

Step 2
.
Let
f
be a bounded continuous function. For each integer
k\geqslant 1
, define
f^{k}=G_{1/k}f
. Then
\{f^{k}\}
is a sequence of bounded continuous functions such that
\sup_{k}\|f^{k}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\leqslant\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}
(see
Lemma

A.3
) and
\lim_{k}f^{k}(z)=f(z)
for every
z\in\mathbb{R}
. We apply (
6.3
) for
f^{k}
to get

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in{D}}\Bigl\|\Bigl\|\int_{S}^{T}\int_{D}X_{r}(y)f^{k}(V_{r}(y)+\psi_{r}(y))dydr\Bigr\|_{{L_{m}}|\mathcal{F}_{S}}\Bigr\|_{L_{n}}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant C\|f^{k}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\Gamma_{X}(T-S)^{1+\frac{1}{4}(\gamma-\frac{1}{p})-\nu}+C\|f^{k}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{p}}\Gamma_{X}[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{\tau,0}L_{m,n}({[S,T]})}}(T-S)^{\frac{3}{4}+\frac{1}{4}(\gamma-\frac{1}{p})+\tau-\nu}.

We now send
k\to\infty
, using the dominated convergence theorem and the properties of the sequence
\{f^{k}\}
described previously to obtain (
6.3
) for the function
f
.
∎

Now we can finally give the proof of
5.2
.

Proof of
5.2
.

The result follows immediately from
6.3
and (
3.2
) by choosing appropriate kernel
X
.

In part (i), we choose
X_{r}(y)=p_{T-r}(x,y)
which satisfies condition (
6.2
) with
\nu=0
and
\Gamma_{X}=1
.

In part (ii), we choose
X_{r}(y)=p_{T-r}(x_{1},y)-p_{T-r}(x_{2},y)
,
n=m
. In this case, thanks to (
C.2
), condition (
6.2
) holds with
\nu=\delta/2
and
\Gamma_{X}=C|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\delta}
.

In part (iii), we choose
X_{r}(y)=p_{T+\bar{T}-r}(x,y)-p_{T-r}(x,y)
,
n=m
. In this case, thanks to (
C.4
), condition (
6.2
) holds with
\nu=\delta/2
and
\Gamma_{X}=C\bar{T}^{\delta/2}
.
∎

6.2
Proof of Lemmas
5.5
,
5.6
and
5.7

Now let us move to the proof of the key lemmas needed for the uniqueness proof.

Proof of
5.5
.

Fix
x\in{D}
,
(s,t)\in\Delta_{0,T_{0}}
,
\beta^{\prime}\in(-2,\beta)
. For any
k,l\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
we apply
6.2
1
with
f=b_{k}-b_{l}
,
\gamma=\beta^{\prime}
,
p=q
,
T=t
,
X_{r}(y)=p_{t-r}(x,y)
,
\Gamma_{X}=1
,
\nu=0
,
\kappa=\psi_{s}
. Note that
\psi_{s}\in{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}
by (
5.28
). Recalling (
3.2
), we get that there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
k,l
such that

\|H^{l,\psi}_{s,t}(x)-H^{k,\psi}_{s,t}(x)\|_{{L_{m}}}\leqslant C(1+T_{0})\|b_{l}-b_{k}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta^{\prime}}_{q}}.

Since the sequence
(b_{n})
converges in
\mathcal{B}^{\beta^{\prime}}_{q}
, we see that the right-hand side of the above inequality tends to
0
as
n,k\to\infty
. Hence the sequence of random variables

(H^{k,\psi}_{s,t}(x))_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is Cauchy in
{L_{m}}
. Thus this sequence converges in
{L_{m}}
(and hence in probability). We denote its limit by
H^{\psi}_{s,t}(x)
.

By exactly the same argument, we see that the sequence

(H^{k,\varphi}_{s,t}(x))_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
converges in probability to a limit which we denote by
H^{\varphi}_{s,t}(x)
.
Now, applying the Fatou’s lemma we derive for each
x\in{D}
,
(s,t)\in\Delta_{0,T_{0}}
,
\lambda\in[0,1]

\displaystyle\|H^{\psi}_{s,t}(x)-H^{\varphi}_{s,t}(x)\|_{{L_{m}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\liminf_{k\to\infty}\|H^{k,\psi}_{s,t}(x)-H^{k,\varphi}_{s,t}(x)\|_{{L_{m}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\|b_{k}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}\|\psi_{s}-\varphi_{s}\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}^{\lambda}(t-s)^{1-\frac{\lambda}{4}+\frac{\beta}{4}-\frac{1}{4q}},

where the second inequality follows from
6.2
2
with

f=b_{k}
,
\gamma=\beta
,
p=q
,

\kappa_{1}=\varphi_{s}
,
\kappa_{2}=\psi_{s}
. Taking into account that by assumption
\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\|b_{k}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}<\infty
and
\beta-1/q\geqslant-1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|H^{\psi}_{s,t}(x)-H^{\varphi}_{s,t}(x)\|_{{L_{m}}}\leqslant C(1+T_{0})\|\psi_{s}-\varphi_{s}\|_{{\mathbf{B}L_{m}}}^{\lambda}(t-s)^{\frac{3}{4}-\frac{\lambda}{4}}.

Now by taking
\lambda=1
in this bound we get (
5.30
), and by taking
\lambda=0
we get (
5.31
).
∎

Proof of
Lemma

5.6
.

Since
u
belongs to
\mathcal{V}(3/4)
and
\sup_{n}\|b_{n}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}<\infty
, we obtain from
5.2
(i) that

\sup_{x\in{D}}\|K^{b_{n};u}_{s}(x)\|_{{L_{m}}}\leqslant C(1+[\psi]_{{\mathcal{C}^{3/4,0}L_{m}({[0,T_{0}]})}})(1+T_{0}).

Therefore for any fixed
x\in{D}
the sequence
(K^{b_{n};u}_{s}(x))_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is uniformly integrable. Recalling that
K^{b_{n};u}_{s}(x)
converges to
K^{u}_{s}(x)
in probability, we get

\|K^{b_{n};u}_{s}(x)-K^{u}_{s}(x)\|_{L_{1}}\to 0\,\,\,\text{as $n\to\infty$}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\|P_{t-s}K^{b_{n};u}_{s}(x)-P_{t-s}K^{u}_{s}(x)\|_{L_{1}}

\displaystyle=\Bigl\|\int_{{D}}p_{t-s}(x,y)(K^{b_{n};u}_{s}(y)-K^{u}_{s}(y))\,dy\Bigr\|_{L_{1}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{{D}}p_{t-s}(x,y)\|K^{b_{n};u}_{s}(y)-K^{u}_{s}(y)\|_{L_{1}}\,dy\to 0,

by the dominated convergence theorem (here we once again made use of (
6.2
)). Thus,
P_{t-s}K^{b_{n};u}_{s}(x)\to P_{t-s}K^{u}_{s}(x)
in probability as
n\to\infty
. The convergence of
P_{t-s}K^{b_{n};v}_{s}(x)
to
P_{t-s}K^{v}_{s}(x)
in probability is obtained by exactly the same argument.
∎

Proof of
5.7
.

The proof is based on the stochastic sewing lemma with critical exponent,
4.5
. Let us verify that all the conditions of this theorem are satisfied. A key tool for the verification will be
6.2
. Fix

0\leqslant S\leqslant T
,
x\in{D}
,
\tau>1/2
. Recall that we are given a sequence of smooth functions

(b_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
such that
b_{k}\to b
in
\mathcal{B}^{\beta-}_{q}
and
\|b_{k}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}\leqslant\|b\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{q}}
for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
.

We put for
(s,t)\in\Delta_{S,T}
,
x\in{D}
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}

A^{k}_{s,t}:=\int_{s}^{t}\int_{D}p_{T-r}(x,y)\bigl[b_{k}(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-s}\psi_{s}(y))-b_{k}(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-s}\varphi_{s}(y))\bigr]\,dydr.

We note that
P_{r-s}\psi_{s}(y)
and
P_{r-s}\varphi_{s}(y)
are well-defined thanks to
B.4
and (
5.28
).

Put for
(t,x)\in[S,T]\times{D}

\mathcal{A}^{k}_{t}=\int_{S}^{t}\int_{D}p_{T-r}(x,y)\bigl[b_{k}(V_{r}(y)+\psi_{r}(y))-b_{k}(V_{r}(y)+\varphi_{r}(y))\bigr]\,dydr.

From now on we fix
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, and will drop the superindex
k
in
A^{k}
and
\mathcal{A}^{k}
.

Let us verify that all the conditions of
4.5
are satisfied. We get for any
S\leqslant s<u<t\leqslant T

\displaystyle\delta A_{s,u,t}=\int_{u}^{t}\int_{D}

\displaystyle p_{T-r}(x,y)\Bigl[b_{k}(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-s}\psi_{s}(y))-b_{k}(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-s}\varphi_{s}(y))

\displaystyle-b_{k}(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-u}\psi_{u}(y))+b_{k}(V_{r}(y)+P_{r-u}\varphi_{u}(y))\Bigr]\,dydr.

We begin by verifying (
4.2
). For fixed
S\leqslant s<u<r\leqslant T
,
y\in{D}
introduce functions
h_{r,y},l_{r,y}\colon\mathbb{R}\times\Omega\to\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle h_{r,y}\colon(z,\omega)\mapsto b_{k}(z+P_{r-s}\psi_{s}(y))-b_{k}(z+P_{r-s}\varphi_{s}(y))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-b_{k}(z+P_{r-u}\psi_{u}(y))+b_{k}(z+P_{r-u}\psi_{u}(y)+P_{r-s}\varphi_{s}(y)-P_{r-s}\psi_{s}(y));

\displaystyle l_{r,y}\colon(z,\omega)\mapsto b_{k}(z+P_{r-u}\varphi_{u}(y))-b_{k}(z+P_{r-u}\psi_{u}(y)+P_{r-s}\varphi_{s}(y)-P_{r-s}\psi_{s}(y)).

Clearly, for fixed non-random parameters the random variables
h_{r,y}(z)
,
l_{r,y}(z)
are
\mathcal{F}_{u}
-measurable. Hence, by (
C.9
) with
u,r
in place of
s,t
, respectively, we deduce for any
\lambda\in[0,1]

\displaystyle|\hskip 0.6458pt\mathsf{E}\hskip 0.43057pt^{s}\delta A_{s,u,t}|
